### Phase 15 — Closing the ledger: the deferred-work sweep, and the public-API baseline gate (2026-09-12)

Every ROADMAP 0.3 phase ended by naming what it left undone, and by Phase 14 that list had been
carried forward thirteen times. This phase closes it. Not by re-opening the three large design
questions — address-equality surgery, cross-process filesystem locking, and Redis's position
representation each stay parked, each having been examined and re-ruled at least three times — but by
shipping every item that is genuinely still open, genuinely small, and genuinely decided.

A research pass over the whole tree at HEAD `8bacb7e` found what remains and, just as usefully, what
does not: **`src` and `tests` contain zero `TODO`, zero `FIXME` and no "not fixed" marker.** Nothing
open lives in the code as a marker. It lives in the handover and in this spec's five
`#### Explicitly parked, with reasons` sections, and it splits cleanly into four documentation claims
that are now false, five Phase 14 Minors that are cheapest to close while the migrations code is
still fresh, one real provider asymmetry, one unbounded collection that two documentation pages
promise a drain for, a handful of one-line debts from Phases 6 and 7 that survived only because
nothing ever swept them, and one genuine feature: the public-API baseline gate, which is the **last
unshipped ROADMAP 0.2 bullet** (`ROADMAP.md:36`).

Nine pieces of work in dependency order, the last of them the close-out. The documentation pass runs
**first**, so no later task's report cites a sentence that is about to change; the baseline gate runs
**second-to-last**, because it must see every other task's public-surface change before it takes a
snapshot of what the surface is allowed to be.

#### 1. The documentation truth pass

Three sentences in the documentation are false or arithmetically stale today, and one true thing is
undocumented. All four are corrected in one commit that touches no code.

- **`CHANGELOG.md:37-38` contradicts `CHANGELOG.md:56`, in the same `### Added` section.** The first
  says the Entity Framework Core outbox package is "Tested against SQLite only; there is no live SQL
  Server job in CI, which is recorded as a stated limitation in `docs/providers/index.md`". The
  second says "Both Entity Framework Core packages now run their full test suites against live SQL
  Server and PostgreSQL instances in CI, alongside SQLite." The second is true —
  `.github/workflows/ci.yml:95` and `:152` both run
  `Statesman.Outbox.EntityFrameworkCore.Tests` — and the first is a Phase 10 sentence that Phase 12
  superseded. Worse, the limitation it cites **no longer exists in `docs/providers/index.md` at
  all**, so the entry cites a statement that was removed. The stale clause goes; the entry keeps its
  shape.
- **`docs/guides/outbox.md:11` promises two provider-specific residuals and lists one.** The
  paragraph ends "…for one reason the outbox inherits and cannot fix, plus two provider-specific
  residuals:", and the two bullets that follow are prune loss (`:13`, which the bullet's own text
  calls "uniform … on every provider" — that is the *inherited* reason, not a provider-specific
  residual) and the lease residual (`:14`). `:16` then correctly says "with one residual still
  standing". Phase 10's change-log fsync closed the second one. The arithmetic is stale in exactly
  one clause.
- **Redis `ImportAsync` is not safe against itself concurrently, and nothing says so.** Phase 13's
  final review found it and parked it as a backlog note (this spec, `:2741-2753`):
  `RedisStateLedgerStore.cs:309-312` reads the member at the incoming revision's score on the plain
  `_database` connection **before** the transaction opens — it has to, because a read queued inside
  `ITransaction` does not resolve until `ExecuteAsync` — and `:322-327` then guards the transaction
  with `Condition.StringEqual(revisionKey, current.Revision)`. Two concurrent imports carrying the
  same revision to different positions both observe the same `current.Revision`, both pass the guard,
  and the loser's member-exact removal targets the member it pre-read, missing the winner's feed
  member, which is left without a history twin. **This phase documents it rather than fixing it**,
  option (i) of the three the parked note lists, in one sentence placed where the Redis provider's
  import caveats already live (`docs/providers/index.md:40`). The real fix — moving the read and the
  removal into one Lua script, mirroring Phase 8's `AppendScript` — stays parked, below.
- **The two maintenance-failure doc sentences** are corrected by item 4, which owns the code they
  describe.

Docs only. No test. `python3 eng/validate.py --report artifacts/static-validation.txt` must stay
PASS, which is the only gate a documentation change has here — it walks `docs/` and
`docs/superpowers` and checks every relative markdown link.

#### 2. The migrations-assembly Minors

Phase 14's Task 1 deferred five Minors on the two subclass-tolerant `IMigrationsAssembly` types. They
are cheapest to close now, while the mechanism is still fresh, and **every one of them is mirrored in
both packages** — `Statesman.Persistence.EntityFrameworkCore/StatesmanMigrationsAssembly.cs` and
`Statesman.Outbox.EntityFrameworkCore/StatesmanOutboxMigrationsAssembly.cs`. Phase 14's final review
found a missing outbox twin as its Minor 4; mirroring here is mandatory, not optional.

**(a) The unreachable null guard is deleted.** `StatesmanMigrationsAssembly.cs:60` and
`StatesmanOutboxMigrationsAssembly.cs:65` both call `ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(currentContext);`
as the first statement of the derived constructor **body**, which runs after
`: base(currentContext, options, idGenerator, logger)`. The base constructor dereferences
`currentContext.Context.GetType()`, so a null argument throws a raw `NullReferenceException` from the
base before the guard is ever reached. It is dead code. **No test is possible and none is written** —
the behaviour cannot be pinned, which is exactly why the correct outcome is deletion, with a one-line
comment in each file recording that a guard cannot run ahead of the base constructor.

**(b) `DeclaredForThisContext` stops throwing `AmbiguousMatchException`.**
`StatesmanMigrationsAssembly.cs:131` and `StatesmanOutboxMigrationsAssembly.cs:136` read
`candidate.GetCustomAttribute<DbContextAttribute>()?.ContextType`. The single-attribute
`GetCustomAttribute<T>()` overload defaults to `inherit: true`, so a migration class hierarchy
carrying `[DbContext]` at two levels yields two matches and throws. Entity Framework Core's own
`GetDbContextType` walks the hierarchy one level at a time with `inherit: false`, which cannot throw.
The replacement is therefore **strictly less tolerant than the base class it replaces** on this one
input, which is a regression the Phase 14 seam was supposed to remove rather than introduce. The fix
is the hierarchy walk. **RED at baseline is provable** — today's code throws — so it gets a test per
package: a migration type deriving from a `[DbContext(typeof(StatesmanLedgerDbContext))]`-attributed
abstract base and carrying its own `[DbContext]`.

**(c) A context-targeted migration with no `[Migration]` attribute is now diagnosed.**
`StatesmanMigrationsAssembly.cs:80-85` and `StatesmanOutboxMigrationsAssembly.cs:85-90` read
`GetCustomAttribute<MigrationAttribute>()?.Id` and `continue` on null, silently. Entity Framework
Core's base class logs `MigrationAttributeMissingWarning` for exactly this case.

**Measured this session, and the measurement is what makes this item shippable rather than a
comment.** `Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Diagnostics.RelationalLoggerExtensions` is `public`; its
`MigrationAttributeMissingWarning(IDiagnosticsLogger<DbLoggerCategory.Migrations>, TypeInfo)`
overload is `public`; neither the type, the method, `IDiagnosticsLogger<>`, nor
`RelationalEventId.MigrationAttributeMissingWarning` carries `[EntityFrameworkInternal]`; and a call
site in the exact shape the derived type needs compiles with **zero warnings** under
`TreatWarningsAsErrors=true` — proven non-vacuous by a control in the same project, a
`MigrationsAssembly` subclass, which failed with two `EF1001` errors in the same build. **So this
adds no second `EF1001` site**, and the phase's entire internal-API surface stays where Phase 14 left
it.

Two further measured facts the implementer needs. First, the logger the extension wants is
**exactly** the `IDiagnosticsLogger<DbLoggerCategory.Migrations>` the constructor already receives —
unlike `CoreLoggerExtensions.TypeLoadingErrorWarning`, whose different category is the reason the
`ReflectionTypeLoadException` fallback in the same file degrades silently (addendum decision 36).
Those two situations are **not** analogous and the new comment must not imply they are. Second,
Entity Framework Core's `MigrationsAssembly` exposes only `Migrations`, `ModelSnapshot` and
`Assembly` — it keeps its logger in a private field — so each derived type stores the constructor's
`logger` in a private field of its own.

**(d) A negative isolation test, per package.** The claim that "an unrelated context is not
[accepted], so a database holding two contexts' migrations in one assembly still keeps them apart" is
asserted only in a source comment (`StatesmanMigrationsAssembly.cs:125-127`).
`EntityFrameworkMigrationsSeamTests.cs` declares two contexts and both derive from
`StatesmanLedgerDbContext`; nothing constructs a context outside that hierarchy. The test is a third
context deriving from `DbContext` directly, given the same migrations assembly, asserting
`Assert.Empty(assembly.Migrations)`. **Its break-the-mechanism lever is specific and must be named:**
replace `declared.IsAssignableFrom(_contextType)` with `true`. Phase 14's own levers — removing
`ReplaceService`, reverting `IsAssignableFrom` to `==` — do **not** discriminate this test.

**(e) The test harness gains an Entity Framework Core logger sink.** Phase 14 amended this to "not
worth closing for one warning" (this spec's C-list entry, and the final review's probe H captured
that warning through `SqlConnection.InfoMessage` instead). Item (c) changes the arithmetic: a
diagnostic nothing in the harness can observe is not a testable claim. Measured: the warning surfaces
through `LogTo(action, [DbLoggerCategory.Migrations.Name])` as

```
warn: … RelationalEventId.MigrationAttributeMissingWarning[20407] (Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Migrations)
      A [Migration] attribute is not specified on the 'NoMigrationAttributeMigration' class.
```

so the assertion is on that message, and the sink is scoped to the migrations category in the one
place that needs it rather than wired globally — matching `EntityFrameworkChangeFeedTests.cs:136`,
the repository's only existing `LogTo` call, which is scoped the same way.

All of it runs on SQLite, SQL Server and PostgreSQL, with `STATESMAN_TEST_EF_RETRY` off and on.

#### 3. The filesystem partition catalog stops listing phantoms

`FileSystemStateLedgerStore.cs:784-799` parses each change-log line and records the highest position
per canonical address, **with no dereference to a stored record**. A torn final line that still
carries five tab-separated fields with parseable numbers parses cleanly and yields a descriptor for an
address the store never wrote. `IStateChangeFeed.ReadAsync` filters the same phantom out, because
dereferencing the record's history file fails. The asymmetry is documented at
`docs/providers/index.md:149` rather than fixed, and has been since Phase 9.

**The mechanism is a filter on the head file existing, never on the history file, and that
distinction is the whole item.** `PruneAsync` (`:346-430`) deletes history revisions and leaves
`head.json` alone — verified: the only `File.Delete` calls in the provider are prune's and two
temp-file cleanups, and no code path deletes a head. So a history-file filter would silently drop a
legitimate partition every one of whose revisions had been pruned: a regression dressed as a fix. The
head file is written by both `AppendAsync` (`:282`) and `ImportAsync` (`:337`), so every genuinely
written address has one, and a torn change-log line never produces one.

Two tests, both RED-provable, and the second is the one that discriminates the correct mechanism from
the wrong one:

1. Append a torn-but-parseable final line to `_changes.log` and assert the address is **not** listed.
2. Prune every revision of a genuinely written address and assert it **is** still listed.

The documented asymmetry at `docs/providers/index.md:149` becomes a description of what the provider
now does.

#### 4. The maintenance-failure queue gets a bound

`StatesmanRuntime.cs:12` declares `private readonly ConcurrentQueue<Exception> _maintenanceFailures = new();`.
`:416`'s `ReportMaintenanceFailure` only ever `Enqueue`s. `:49` exposes `MaintenanceFailures` as
**`internal`**, and as a `ToArray()` copy — never a dequeue. A repository-wide search finds **zero
consumers** in `src` or `tests`. The only caller of the reporter is `StateHandle.cs:678`, on the
post-append prune path, which `StateHandle` runs after **every** successful append. So a store whose
prune keeps failing retains one `Exception` per append for the lifetime of the runtime, with no API
through which an application could read or clear it.

And two documentation pages tell applications to drain it:
`docs/operations/observability.md:21` ("Applications should drain or surface those diagnostics through
their normal logging and health infrastructure") and `docs/reference/diagnostics.md:29`.
`ROADMAP.md:31` has carried "rate-limited maintenance failure reporting" since 0.2.

This phase bounds the retention — the most recent N, plus a count of how many were dropped — and
corrects both documentation sentences so they describe what exists. `_degradedMaintenanceStores` is
keyed by store name and is already bounded; only the queue is not.

**Adding a public surface is explicitly out of scope** and is parked below. One of its three shapes
would put a logging dependency into the core `Statesman` package, which has **zero** external
dependencies beyond `Statesman.Abstractions` — an invariant preserved through fourteen phases — so it
is a maintainer's call, not a sweep item. The bound itself touches no public surface and no storage
format, and the core package's dependency set does not change.

RED at baseline is trivial: today the count grows without limit.

#### 5. The outbox sweep

Seven small items across four source files and two test files, all of them Phase 6 or Phase 7
residue. Two of them may correctly land as a comment rather than as code, and the section says so
rather than mandating a change.

- **A standby replica becomes visible in logs.** `StatesmanOutboxHostedService.cs:167` sets
  `standby = result.Outcome == OutboxDispatchOutcome.LeaseUnavailable;` and nothing logs it. Both
  neighbouring outcomes do: `LeaseLost` at `:169-175` (`LogWarning`) and `Skipped` at `:177+`
  (`LogError`). A deployment in which every replica is standby, or in which takeover silently never
  happens, produces no log line at all. The fix logs the **transition into** standby and the
  **transition out**, and a steady standby logs nothing further — that third assertion is what stops
  the fix becoming one log line per `PollInterval`.
  `tests/Statesman.Outbox.Tests/RecordingLogger.cs` already exists.
- **The cleared-record test asserts the property its name implies.**
  `StateChangeMessageTests.cs:69-77`, `A_cleared_record_carries_no_payload_and_no_content_type`,
  asserts `Assert.Null(message.Payload)` and `Assert.Null(message.ContentType)` and never asserts the
  operation survived as `StateOperation.Cleared`. One added assertion.
- **Redis's `ListPartitionsAsync` honours its token before the fetch, and stops duplicating its own
  deserializer.** `RedisStateLedgerStore.cs:670-685`: `HashGetAllAsync(PartitionsKey())` runs at
  `:673` with no cancellation check above it — the check is at `:675`, inside the yield loop, after
  the whole hash has been fetched — and `:677` inlines
  `JsonSerializer.Deserialize<RedisPartitionEntry>` where the private `DeserializePartition` helper
  already exists at `:829` and is used at `:297`. **Measured RED, with no live Redis needed:** a store
  over a multiplexer configured `AbortOnConnectFail = false` against an unreachable endpoint, given an
  already-cancelled token, today throws
  `StackExchange.Redis.RedisConnectionException: The message timed out in the backlog … command=HGETALL`.
  The `command=HGETALL` is the proof that the fetch was issued before the token was honoured. After
  the fix it must be `OperationCanceledException`.
  **Not included:** replacing `HGETALL` with `HashScanAsync`, which is a server-behaviour change on a
  possibly-large key and belongs with the cluster work, parked below.
- **`RedisOutboxCursorStore`'s token contract is settled either way.**
  `RedisOutboxCursorStore.cs:58` and `:77` are its only two uses of `cancellationToken`, both
  `ThrowIfCancellationRequested()` at method entry. The honest caveat: the two Redis calls it makes
  are `StringGetAsync` and `ScriptEvaluateAsync`, and **neither takes a `CancellationToken`**, so
  "pass the token to Redis" is not an available option. The two shippable outcomes are a
  `.WaitAsync(cancellationToken)` wrapper — which abandons the in-flight command rather than
  cancelling it, and must say so — or one sentence of documentation stating that the token is honoured
  at entry only. **The implementer measures StackExchange.Redis's own surface before promising
  either, and records which they chose and why.** This is the one item where a comment may be the
  correct answer.

  *Amended (Task 9, 2026-09-12):* shipped as the **comment**. Measured by reflection over
  `StackExchange.Redis.IDatabaseAsync` 3.1.31: neither `StringGetAsync` nor `ScriptEvaluateAsync` has a
  `CancellationToken` overload in that version. A `<para>` was added to `RedisOutboxCursorStore`'s
  class-level `<remarks>` stating the token is honoured at entry only, and why a `.WaitAsync` wrapper
  was rejected — it would abandon rather than cancel the in-flight command, a behaviour change with no
  measured benefit. No test added, no code change.
- **`Deduplicated` gets a contract or an `Interlocked`, on evidence.**
  `RedisStreamStateChangeSink.cs:82` declares `public long Deduplicated { get; private set; }`,
  incremented at `:121` with `Deduplicated++`. `StateChangeDispatcher` publishes serially, so there is
  no live race today. The correct outcome is probably a documented single-writer contract on the
  property rather than `Interlocked`; the implementer's call, on evidence, recorded either way.

  *Amended (Task 9, 2026-09-12):* shipped as the **documented single-writer contract**. Verified
  directly against `StateChangeDispatcher.cs:419`: exactly one call site, `await
  _sink.PublishAsync(batch, cancellationToken)`, inside the dispatcher's one serial awaited publish
  loop — no concurrent call site exists. The brief's exact `<remarks>` block was applied verbatim to
  `Deduplicated`; no `Interlocked` was added.
- **`FileSystemOutboxCursorStore`'s never-evicted gates are documented, and its triple flush is
  measured before it is touched.** `:29` holds a
  `static readonly ConcurrentDictionary<string, SemaphoreSlim> Gates`, one entry per distinct resolved
  file path, never disposed and never evicted — which is correct for a process-lifetime store keyed by
  a bounded set of outbox ids, and is worth a comment saying so rather than a change. The flush is the
  other half: `:74` sets `Options = FileOptions.WriteThrough`, `:83` awaits `stream.FlushAsync(...)`,
  and `:84` calls `stream.Flush(flushToDisk: true)` — **three durability calls for one small write.**
  This is a durability path, so Phase 11's and Phase 12's rulings both bind: `fsync` is not black-box
  provable in this repository, and a perf change that measures slower is a regression. The evidence is
  therefore a **committed env-gated measurement** — `STATESMAN_MEASURE_OUTBOX_CURSOR_WRITE=1`,
  following the `STATESMAN_MEASURE_FEED_SCAN` precedent — reporting before and after in **Debug and
  Release** over 200 writes, plus the existing round-trip tests left unedited. The calls collapse to
  the fewest that still fsync **only if that measures faster**; otherwise the outcome is a comment
  recording the measurement, and the three calls stay.

  *Amended (Task 9, 2026-09-12):* shipped as the **comment**; the calls did not collapse. Measured over
  200 writes: Debug three-call 6.737ms median / 8.088ms p95 (a first-pass outlier — see below), Release
  three-call 1.933ms / 2.877ms; Debug two-call (candidate, `FlushAsync` dropped) 1.770ms / 6.453ms,
  Release two-call 1.794ms / 2.598ms. Three further re-runs per form and configuration were taken
  because the first Debug three-call reading was a clear outlier against everything else measured:
  once warmed, both forms converge to ~1.8–1.9ms median in Debug (1.886/1.964/1.927ms two-call versus
  1.851/1.938/1.819ms three-call), and Release showed no separation in the first pair either. The
  collapse is **not measurably faster in either configuration**, so the three-call form was kept, with
  a comment above the `FileStreamOptions` construction recording the measurement, and
  `STATESMAN_MEASURE_OUTBOX_CURSOR_WRITE` ships as a permanent harness for the next time this question
  is asked.
- **`OutboxCursorFile` becomes `internal`.**
  `FileSystemOutboxCursorStore.cs:156` declares `public sealed record OutboxCursorFile(string OutboxId, long Position);`
  — the on-disk shape of the cursor file, with no consumer anywhere in `src` or `tests`.
  `Statesman.Outbox` shipped in `v0.3.0`, so removing it from the public surface is **breaking**. It is
  taken deliberately, in the same `0.4.0-alpha` window that justified the `IStateChangeFeed.ReadAsync`
  break (`CHANGELOG.md:7-24`), and item 8's suppression file is where it is recorded. Doing it in this
  phase rather than later is the point: item 8 freezes the surface, so a break not taken now is a
  break that needs a second suppression entry later.

#### 6. Tooling's two missing failure tests

`tests/Statesman.Tooling.Tests/StateLedgerRestoreTests.cs` holds ten facts and **every failure case is
a validation refusal that happens before the target is contacted**. Nothing covers a target that
accepts N records and then throws — which is the case the whole validate-first design exists to
bound, and therefore the one case whose behaviour a reader most needs pinned. The test is a store
double whose `ImportAsync` throws on the Nth call; it asserts what the target holds afterwards and
pins **a partially restored target** as the documented behaviour.

`StateLedgerExportTests.cs` holds six facts, none exercising cancellation mid-enumeration.
`ExportAsync` writes the header before it enumerates any history
(`StateLedgerExport.cs:98-115`) and checks the token once per record, and `ExportToFileAsync` writes
to `path + ".tmp"` and deletes it on any exception (`:146-166`), so cancelling after the header must
leave **neither** file at the destination. The existing
`ExportToFileAsync_leaves_the_previous_file_intact_when_the_export_fails` covers the failure case;
this is the cheap cancellation twin.

Test-only, fully independent, and it may run in parallel with items 3, 4 and 5. If either test finds
something, that is a finding, not a scope change — report it.

#### 7. Repository hygiene

Two mechanical items, both of which must be **verified by output rather than by reading**, because in
both cases the question is whether an existing line does anything at all.

- **`Npgsql.EntityFrameworkCore.PostgreSQL` gets a Dependabot minor/patch group.**
  `.github/dependabot.yml`: `dotnet-platform` matches `Microsoft.*`, `test-tooling` matches
  `Microsoft.Testing.*`/`xunit*`/`coverlet*`, `supporting-libraries` matches exactly
  `Nerdbank.GitVersioning` and `StackExchange.Redis`, and `major-updates` matches `*` for majors only.
  `Npgsql.*` therefore has no minor/patch group and its non-major updates arrive ungrouped. Phase 14
  declared this "not Phase 14's to fix" (this spec, `:3113-3116`); it is Phase 15's.
- **`src/Directory.Build.props`'s nine-project exclusion list is resolved.** `:12` is one
  `TargetFrameworks` property whose condition names nine projects, and the comment above it
  (`:5-9`) records both that each of those projects **already** declares its own `<TargetFramework>`,
  which wins (measured, addendum decision 31), and that inverting the pattern "is the better end state
  and is explicitly parked." So the condition states intent rather than carrying it. The risk is real
  though small: the condition is load-bearing only if some project does *not* declare its own target,
  which is why the exit check is a **CI-shaped `dotnet pack` over `src` producing 22 nupkgs with
  unchanged `lib/` target-framework folders per package**, plus `eng/validate.py` still reporting
  `projects: 41`.

#### 8. The public-API baseline gate — the last open 0.2 bullet

`ROADMAP.md:36` has carried *"public API compatibility baselines and package-validation gates"* since
0.2. `Directory.Build.props:12` *(corrected from :21)* sets `<EnablePackageValidation>true</EnablePackageValidation>`, but
there is **no `PackageValidationBaselineVersion` anywhere in the repository and no
`CompatibilitySuppressions.xml` anywhere**, so package validation currently gates nothing across
versions. Tag `v0.3.0` published fifteen packages, which is what finally makes a baseline possible.

**The decision this needed, taken: baseline `0.3.0` for the fifteen, and commit the suppression files
that record the deliberate breaks.** That is the shape the tooling is designed for, it starts
protecting consumers now, and a suppression entry is exactly how a deliberate pre-1.0 break is meant
to be recorded. The cost is that the suppression files become a durable artifact of those breaks —
which is the part worth revisiting if this ruling was wrong. The two alternatives were cutting
`0.4.0` first and baselining against it (which keeps the alpha window free but leaves the gate
unbuilt for another release) and enabling the gate in the non-breaking direction only (which is not a
thing the tooling does).

**Everything below was measured this session against SDK 10.0.303 with live access to nuget.org.**

Fifteen packages are published at `0.3.0` and get a baseline; the other seven —
`Statesman.Outbox.EntityFrameworkCore`, which was added *after* the `v0.3.0` tag, plus the six Phase
14 migration packages — have no `0.3.0` to validate against. Confirmed directly against
`api.nuget.org`: all fifteen resolve `0.3.0`, and all seven return `BlobNotFound`.

**The property lives in `src/Directory.Build.props`, and the seven exclude themselves by blanking it.**
This is not a style choice. The baseline package is fetched by a **restore-time** download, so setting
the property on the `pack` command line fails hard:

```
error MSB4018: System.IO.FileNotFoundException: Package
'C:\Users\jd\.nuget\packages\statesman.outbox\0.3.0\statesman.outbox.0.3.0.nupkg' not found.
```

Measured with the property in `src/Directory.Build.props` instead: `dotnet restore Statesman.slnx`
downloads all fifteen baselines, and the CI-shaped `dotnet pack --no-build` then validates against
them. Each of the seven unbaselined csproj files carries
`<PackageValidationBaselineVersion></PackageValidationBaselineVersion>` with a one-line comment, which
was measured to disable baseline validation for that project alone — all seven packed clean. The end
state when `0.4.0` ships is one property bumped and seven overrides deleted.

**Six packages break against `0.3.0`, nineteen diagnostics in total, and every one of them is the
`ReadAsync` change.** Measured:

| Package | Diagnostics | Shape |
|---|---|---|
| `Statesman.Abstractions` | 6 | `CP0002` + `CP0006`, three target frameworks — the interface both loses the old member and gains a new one |
| `Statesman` | 3 | `CP0002`, three target frameworks |
| `Statesman.Persistence.FileSystem` | 3 | `CP0002`, three target frameworks |
| `Statesman.Persistence.Redis` | 3 | `CP0002`, three target frameworks |
| `Statesman.Persistence.Tiered` | 3 | `CP0002`, three target frameworks |
| `Statesman.Persistence.EntityFrameworkCore` | 1 | `CP0002`, `net10.0` only |

The nine remaining baselined packages pack clean. Only `CP0002` and `CP0006` appear; the
implementing classes raise `CP0002` alone, because adding a method to a class is not a break while
adding one to an interface is.

**The suppression files are generated once and committed, and the property that generates them is
`ApiCompatGenerateSuppressionFile`** — not the older `GenerateCompatibilitySuppressionFile`. They land
one per project at `src/<Project>/CompatibilitySuppressions.xml`, and after item 5's `OutboxCursorFile`
change there will be a seventh, for `Statesman.Outbox`. `eng/validate.py` stays PASS with them present
— measured — because `.xml` is outside its text-hygiene suffix set, so the UTF-8 byte-order mark the
generator writes is inert; the files' only effect on its metrics is `all_files`.

**Each suppression is explained in an adjacent documentation page, never in an XML comment.**
Measured: a hand-added comment inside `CompatibilitySuppressions.xml` **does not survive
regeneration** — the generator rewrites the file whole. A comment would therefore be a note that
silently disappears the next time a deliberate break is taken, which is precisely the moment someone
needs to read it.

**The gate runs on `pack`.** Both workflows that pack — `ci.yml:311-336`'s `pack` job and
`release.yml:37-44` — run `dotnet restore Statesman.slnx` and then
`dotnet pack "$project" --configuration Release --no-build` over `find src -name '*.csproj'`, so both
get the gate with no workflow edit. `ci.yml`'s `pack` job needs `build-test`, `coverage`,
`redis-tests`, `sqlserver-tests` and `postgres-tests`, so a break surfaces after the test suite rather
than instead of it.

**The gate is demonstrated sensitive.** Measured: adding a `bool` parameter ahead of the
`CancellationToken` on `StateLedgerExport.ExportAsync` — a package with no suppression file — fails
`dotnet pack` with

```
error CP0002: Member 'System.Threading.Tasks.ValueTask<Statesman.Tooling.StateLedgerExportSummary>
Statesman.Tooling.StateLedgerExport.ExportAsync(Statesman.IStateLedgerStore, Statesman.StatesmanManifest,
System.IO.Stream, Statesman.Tooling.StateLedgerExportOptions?, System.Threading.CancellationToken)'
exists on [Baseline] lib/net10.0/Statesman.Tooling.dll but not on lib/net10.0/Statesman.Tooling.dll
error : API breaking changes found. If those are intentional, the APICompat suppression file can be
updated by rebuilding with '/p:ApiCompatGenerateSuppressionFile=true'
```

once per target framework, and the scratch change was reverted.

#### 9. Close-out

`CHANGELOG.md` `[Unreleased]` entries for the bound, the catalog fix, the breaking `OutboxCursorFile`
change and the baseline gate; `ROADMAP.md:36` annotated shipped in `:34`'s style; a `HANDOVER.md`
Phase 15 entry in the Phase 14 entry's shape — commits, per-task review outcomes, the mechanisms, and
what was parked and why, with **every number copied from a task report rather than from a brief's
prediction**; the exit criteria below amended from predictions to measured values; the Minors this
phase itself defers carried forward explicitly; and a note that the research inventory under
`.superpowers/sdd/` is scratch and is not tracked.

#### Explicitly parked, with reasons

- **A public surface for maintenance-failure diagnostics.** Item 4 bounds the queue and corrects the
  two documentation sentences; exposing it is a separate call. Of its three shapes — a read-and-clear
  surface on `IStatesman` or a diagnostics type, routing to `ILogger` and deleting the queue, or
  keeping it internal — the second would add a logging dependency the core package deliberately does
  not have, and that constraint belongs in the question rather than in an implementer's judgement.
- **`MessageId` collision from ordinal `Root` versus canonical lower-casing.** Parked identically in
  Phases 5, 10, 11, 12 and 13, always as a 0.4 item needing address-equality surgery across the
  library, and documented at `docs/providers/index.md:199`. `Identity.cs:118` gives `StateAddress`
  compiler-generated ordinal equality on `Root` while `:120`'s `Canonical` lower-cases it, so a store
  written under both `"App"` and `"app"` has one revision sequence on the providers that key by
  `Canonical` and two on Entity Framework Core, which keys by the raw columns. Every fix touches
  *stored* identity: normalizing at construction changes Entity Framework Core row keys, making
  `Canonical` ordinal changes the filesystem provider's per-address directory names and Redis's stream
  keys, and rejecting a differently-cased root narrows the guarantee and needs a per-provider lookup
  on the write path. It cannot be scoped small.
- **Cross-process filesystem append locking.** Parked with an unchanged ruling in Phases 10, 11, 12
  and 13, and Phase 12 strengthened it. `FileSystemStateLedgerStore.cs:66`'s `_globalPosition` is
  allocated by a tick-based `Interlocked.CompareExchange` loop (`:1682-1701`) and is **never seeded
  from the existing log** (`:1262` says so). A sidecar lock would close the byte-interleaving symptom
  while two processes still allocated overlapping positions, and would re-open compaction's design
  (`:444`: "must run in the writer's own process. The provider is single-writer by design").
- **Redis concurrent same-revision `ImportAsync`, options (ii) and (iii).** Item 1 takes option (i),
  the documented sentence. The real fix is a new Lua script on the one write path Phase 8 deliberately
  left alone; a per-address import lock is the third option. Import is a bulk restore path the library
  never drives concurrently against itself, which is what makes documenting it honest rather than
  evasive.
- **Redis Cluster coverage, and `HGETALL` versus `HashScanAsync`.** `ROADMAP.md:33` asks for "Redis
  cluster and script compatibility tests"; the script half **is** covered
  (`tests/Statesman.Redis.Tests/RedisAppendScriptTests.cs`), the cluster half is not —
  `ci.yml:56`'s `redis-tests` runs one standalone Redis and nothing in the repository references a
  cluster, so Phase 4's `CROSSSLOT` handling stays reasoned rather than executed. A fourth
  infrastructure job is not a sweep item, and the `HashScanAsync` conversion should ride with it,
  since a cluster is the environment where a server-blocking `HGETALL` actually matters.
- **Pipelining the Redis sink.** `RedisStreamStateChangeSink.cs:95` awaits `StreamAddAsync` per
  message and `:114` awaits a second read per message for the dedup and lineage check. The open
  question is whether that read — which is what makes Phase 7's lineage-collision guard work — can be
  batched at all; if it cannot, pipelining only the `XADD`s buys little. Any attempt must measure in
  Debug **and** Release (Phase 12 D4) and keep the lineage guard's test.
- **An HTTP webhook sink.** Phase 7 parked it. A new feature and probably a new package.
- **Lifting Redis's `MaxImportablePosition`.** `RedisStateLedgerStore.cs:93` bounds imports at
  `1L << 52` and `:270-274` refuses anything above it, because sorted-set scores are IEEE doubles, so
  a filesystem export — whose positions are UTC ticks, around 6.4e17 — cannot be restored into Redis.
  The fix this spec names at `:570` reshapes the feed to an exact large-position representation, which
  is a storage-format change to the Redis feed.
- **The four unbuilt ROADMAP 0.2 features.** Load diagnostics with per-source timing and health
  summaries; serializer envelopes with content type, serializer id and migration provenance (a
  storage-format change); health checks (a new package dependency); analyzer code fixes and stronger
  collection-mutation data-flow analysis. Each is a new public surface rather than a defect, and each
  needs its own design pass. Verified not started: no `LoadDiagnostic`/`SourceTiming`/`LoadReport`/`HealthSummary`
  type, no `IHealthCheck`, no `SerializerId`, and no `CodeFixProvider` anywhere in `src` or `tests`.
- **Shared conformance coverage for compare-and-swap, cancellation and corruption**, and shared
  conformance for `IPartitionCatalog`, `IDistributedCapture`, `IStateChangeNotifier` and
  `IStateLedgerReplica`. All exist as per-provider tests; lifting them into
  `tests/Statesman.Conformance.Tests` is the remaining half of ROADMAP 0.2's first bullet and is a
  phase of its own.
- **A store-format upgrade or restore-from-corruption runbook.** ROADMAP 0.2's "upgrade and corruption
  scenarios are documented" exit criterion is partial: corruption is documented per provider and
  upgrade is documented for Entity Framework Core schema migration and for declared state migration,
  but there is no operator runbook. Documentation work with a real design question inside it.
- **Filesystem verify, repair and index-rebuild tooling.** Compaction shipped in 0.3
  (`FileSystemStateLedgerStore.cs:444`); `src/Statesman.Tooling/` holds only export, restore and their
  format and exception types.

#### The public API delta

**One breaking change, one type's accessibility, and no capability change.**

`Statesman.Outbox.OutboxCursorFile` — the `public sealed record` at
`FileSystemOutboxCursorStore.cs:156` describing the on-disk cursor file — becomes `internal`. It
shipped in `v0.3.0` with no consumer in `src` or `tests`, and the change is taken deliberately in the
`0.4.0-alpha` window, recorded in `src/Statesman.Outbox/CompatibilitySuppressions.xml` and explained
in the documentation page item 8 adds.

Nothing else changes signature. Items 2, 3, 4 and 5's other changes are internal behaviour,
documentation, tests or comments. Item 8 adds no API at all — it constrains every future one.

**No capability is added, so `docs/architecture/capabilities.md` gains no row and flips no cell, and
`tests/Statesman.Capabilities.Tests/CapabilityMatrixTests.cs` is untouched.** This is the Phase 10
through 14 precedent, stated in every one of those five close-outs.

Two new artifacts that are not API: up to seven `src/<Project>/CompatibilitySuppressions.xml` files,
and one new optional environment variable, `STATESMAN_MEASURE_OUTBOX_CURSOR_WRITE`, joining
`STATESMAN_MEASURE_FEED_SCAN` and `STATESMAN_MEASURE_INMEMORY_DRAIN`.

#### Exit criteria

**Every count below is a prediction made before the phase, and the close-out task replaces it with
the measured value.** Baselines measured at `8bacb7e`, SQLite default, no live Redis, SQL Server or
PostgreSQL, Debug, by running each project's built xUnit v3 executable directly:

| Project | total | succeeded | skipped | failed |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Statesman.Tests` | 95 | 92 | 3 | 0 |
| `Statesman.FileSystem.Tests` | 55 | 51 | 4 | 0 |
| `Statesman.Outbox.Tests` | 80 | 80 | 0 | 0 |
| `Statesman.Tooling.Tests` | 22 | 19 | 3 | 0 |
| `Statesman.EntityFrameworkCore.Tests` | 46 | 33 | 13 | 0 |

### Phase 16 — One conformance suite, and the observability bullet (2026-09-13)

ROADMAP 0.2 has nine bullets. Phase 14 shipped one (Entity Framework Core migrations) and Phase 15
shipped another (public-API baselines), and Phase 15's close-out wrote down, honestly, that **0.2 is
not complete**. This phase takes the two bullets that are about proving behaviour rather than adding
breadth: bullet 1, the shared provider conformance suite, and bullet 3, "OpenTelemetry semantic
conventions, health checks, and rate-limited maintenance failure reporting". Bullet 2 (load
diagnostics with per-source timing and health summaries) was considered and is parked with reasons
below; the health check this phase ships is deliberately shaped so it can consume load diagnostics
later without a break.

**What the pre-flight measurement changed about the plan.** The controller's framing was "lift
compare-and-swap, cancellation and corruption into the shared suite". Measured at `2b04fb5`, three of
those four premises are wrong, and the corrections are what this section is built on:

- **Compare-and-swap is not covered per provider, so there is nothing to lift.** A repository-wide
  read of every `Assert.False(... .Succeeded)` and every occurrence of `Conflict` in `tests/` finds
  exactly two provider-level conditional-write assertions: `RedisChangeFeedTests.cs:300-305` (a
  contested `Absent` under a paused clock) and `EntityFrameworkRetryStrategyTests.cs:217` (the
  lost-acknowledgement conflict, PostgreSQL only). `AbstractionsAndFixtureCoverageTests.cs:157-161`
  reads like coverage and is not: it exercises the `StateAppendResult.Conflict` factory, never a
  store. The in-memory, filesystem and tiered providers have **no** conditional-write test at all. The
  shared suite is therefore new coverage rather than a lift, and **no per-provider test is deleted by
  it** — there is none to delete.
- **The conditional-write contract is already uniform across all five providers.** A direct probe over
  in-memory, filesystem, tiered, Entity Framework Core on SQLite and live Redis produced byte-identical
  rows for every case: `Absent` on an occupied address conflicts with `Record` null and `Current`
  carrying revision 1; a mismatched `AtRevision` does the same; two conflicts leave head and history at
  `[1]`; a matching `AtRevision` yields revision 2; `Any` succeeds on an occupied and on an absent
  address; and eight racing `Absent` appends produce exactly one winner and seven conflicts, all seven
  carrying `Current`. The shared suite is a **pinning** suite, green at baseline, and it needs a
  break-the-mechanism lever rather than a RED-at-baseline proof.
- **Cancellation is likewise already uniform, and likewise almost untested.** The same probe ran an
  already-cancelled token through `ReadLatestAsync`, `ReadHistoryAsync`, `AppendAsync`, `PruneAsync`
  and every capability each provider advertises, across all five providers: **every one of the
  thirty-nine call sites threw `OperationCanceledException`.** The one non-cancellation row was
  informative rather than a defect — tiered-over-in-memory throws `NotSupportedException` naming the
  cold tier for `CaptureAsync`, because in-memory has no `IDistributedCapture`, which is why this
  phase's tiered capture fixture uses an Entity Framework Core cold store. Today the repository pins
  three of those thirty-nine (`TieredLedgerTests.cs:83`, `FileSystemChangeFeedTests.cs:218`,
  `RedisPartitionCatalogTests.cs:43`). Again: pinning suite, lever not RED.
- **Corruption genuinely differs per provider, so the shared contract is written at the level they
  share.** Only the filesystem provider has a textual on-disk record a torn write can damage, and its
  four corruption tests (`FileSystemChangeFeedTests.cs:449`, `:513`, `:545`,
  `FileSystemPartitionCatalogTests.cs:113`, plus `FileSystemChangeLogGenerationTests.cs:174`) are
  about that specific surface. What all four import targets share is the **refusal** half: every
  `ImportAsync` calls `StateRecord.Validate()` before touching the store
  (`InMemoryStateLedgerStore.cs:225`, `FileSystemStateLedgerStore.cs:294`,
  `RedisStateLedgerStore.cs:269`, `EntityFrameworkStateLedgerStore.cs:605`). That is the shared
  corruption contract this phase writes down, and the provider-specific reader behaviour stays exactly
  where it is, with the reason recorded rather than left as an omission.

#### 1. The provider fixture: one construction point for every suite

`tests/Statesman.Conformance.Tests/` today constructs each provider's store inline in each subclass,
which is fine for two suites and would be seven copies of five constructions by the end of this phase.
A `ConformanceProviders` static class becomes the single construction point — one factory per provider,
each returning the existing `ConformanceStore` and carrying that provider's skip reason — and the seven
shipped subclasses re-point at it while keeping their own provider-specific constants (`PauseCallIndex`,
`LeaseTtl`, `RejectsCollidingPositions`, `Maintain`). The Entity Framework Core factory takes an
`EntityFrameworkTestConcurrency` argument, because the change-feed suite needs concurrent transactions
and the lease suite deliberately does not.

This item changes no behaviour and no test count. Its exit check is exactly that: `Statesman.Conformance.Tests`
`total: 75`, `failed: 0`, before and after, with the same twenty skips.

#### 2. Compare-and-swap and revision conflict, shared

`LedgerWriteConformanceTests`, run once per provider, pinning the six properties the probe measured as
uniform. The exclusion assertion follows `LeaseConformanceTests`' precedent exactly: the racing-append
assertion is a `public static` method so a deliberately broken store can be required to fail it.

The break-the-mechanism proof is `BrokenStoreConformanceTests`, modelled on `BrokenLeaseProviderTests`:
a store double whose `AppendAsync` ignores its `StateWriteCondition` entirely must fail the shared
assertion, and a double that honours it must pass. Both halves, because a suite that failed against
everything would be as useless as one that passed against everything.

#### 3. Cancellation, shared

`CancellationConformanceTests`, run once per provider. Three properties: an already-cancelled token is
honoured by all four `IStateLedgerStore` operations; it is honoured by every capability the provider
advertises, each reached through `TryGetCapability` so a provider that honestly answers "No" skips
rather than silently passes; and a cancelled append leaves head and history unchanged. The negative
half lives in the same `BrokenStoreConformanceTests` file: a store double that swallows its token must
fail the shared assertion.

This is the first place in the repository where the cancellation contract is stated as a contract
rather than as three incidental per-provider tests, and it is what would catch a provider that grew a
new capability and forgot the token — which is exactly the Phase 15 defect
`RedisPartitionCatalogTests.cs:43` pins after the fact.

#### 4. Corruption, at the level the providers share: what an import refuses

`ImportRejectionConformanceTests`, run once per provider, with the tiered store skipping honestly
because it vetoes `IStateLedgerReplica` outright. Three properties: a record that fails
`StateRecord.Validate()` is refused; a null record is refused; and a refused import leaves the change
feed and the partition catalog exactly as they were. The third is the one that discriminates — a
provider that validated after writing would pass the first two.

**Provider-specific corruption stays per provider, deliberately.** The filesystem provider's torn-line
and corrupt-generation-file tests describe a surface no other provider has: an append-only text log
that a crash can leave half-written. Lifting them would mean either inventing a damage mechanism for
providers whose storage cannot be damaged that way, or relaxing the shared assertion until it asserted
nothing. `docs/providers/index.md`'s "Partition catalog semantics and limitations" section already
documents the per-provider difference; this phase adds the sentence saying why the shared suite stops
where it does.

#### 5. `IPartitionCatalog` and `IStateLedgerReplica`, shared

Two suites, each run once per provider. The catalog suite pins one descriptor per distinct address, each
at that address's newest position, nothing for a store never written to, and a partition created by an
import appearing in the listing. The replica suite pins exactness (the record is stored with its
original `GlobalPosition`), idempotence for the same address and revision, no head regression when an
older revision arrives later, and that an append after an import never reuses an imported position.

Both suites' capability presence is answered by `TryGetCapability` at the test site, never by a
per-provider boolean: the tiered store's honest "No" on `IStateLedgerReplica` is a skip with a reason,
and a provider that lost the capability would start skipping loudly rather than passing quietly.

#### 6. `IStateChangeNotifier` and `IDistributedCapture`, shared

Two suites with four honest "No" cells between them — the filesystem and Entity Framework Core providers
have no notifier, and the in-memory and filesystem providers have no distributed capture. The notifier
suite pins that an append signals a live subscriber, that disposing the store ends every live
subscription, and that a consumer which ignores every hint still reads every record from the feed (the
contract's own escape clause, and the one a provider is most likely to break by making a hint carry
data). The capture suite pins that an empty address set returns an empty result without contacting the
store, that every requested address gets an entry including the absent ones, and that an out-of-domain
consistency level is a caller error rather than a silent downgrade.

The tiered capture subclass uses an **Entity Framework Core cold store**, not the in-memory cold store
the other tiered subclasses use: measured, tiered-over-in-memory throws `NotSupportedException` naming
the cold tier, because `CaptureAsync` always delegates to cold and in-memory has no capture. A tiered
store's capture behaviour is only observable over a capture-capable cold tier.

#### 7. The maintenance-failure diagnostics surface, rate-limited

ROADMAP 0.2 bullet 3's third clause. Phase 15 bounded the retained collection at 64 and added
`statesman.maintenance.failures` and `statesman.maintenance.failures.dropped`, and parked the public
surface as a maintainer's call because one of the three candidate shapes would have added a logging
dependency the core package deliberately does not have. This phase takes option (i) — a public,
read-and-clear diagnostics surface — because it is the only one of the three that makes the two
documentation pages true without touching the dependency invariant.

**The shape is discovery, not a new `IStatesman` member.** `IStatesman` is a published interface in a
baselined package; adding a member to it is a `CP0006` break for any external implementer. Instead,
`IStatesmanDiagnostics` is a new interface in `Statesman.Abstractions`, `StatesmanRuntime` implements
it, and `StatesmanDiagnosticsExtensions.TryGetDiagnostics(this IStatesman, out IStatesmanDiagnostics?)`
is how a caller reaches it. That is the repository's own `TryGetCapability` convention applied one level
up, and it is purely additive. `IStatesmanDiagnostics` deliberately does **not** extend
`IStateCapability` — precisely as `IStateCapabilityProvider` deliberately does not — because
`CapabilityMatrixTests`' completeness half reflects over every `IStateCapability` implementer and would
demand a matrix row for something that is not a store capability. A test pins that it does not.

**What rate-limiting means here, stated rather than implied.** The limiter gates **retention**, per
source, and never gates acceptance: an append is authoritative whether or not its prune failed, and
that does not change. Each store name gets a token bucket of sixteen tokens refilling completely every
minute, judged against the runtime's injected `TimeProvider`, so a test drives it with
`ManualTimeProvider` at default options and never pins an interval to zero. A failure the bucket admits
is retained, subject to the existing 64 bound. A failure the bucket refuses is not retained and is
counted on a new `statesman.maintenance.failures.suppressed`. The three counters then have disjoint,
stateable meanings:

| Counter | Counts |
|---|---|
| `statesman.maintenance.failures` | every reported failure, unchanged |
| `statesman.maintenance.failures.suppressed` | failures the per-source rate limiter refused to retain |
| `statesman.maintenance.failures.dropped` | retained failures the 64 bound later evicted |

Sixteen per minute rather than sixty-four is deliberate: a limit equal to the retention bound would
make `dropped` unreachable within a single window and collapse two independent mechanisms into one
number. With sixteen, a single source's burst is throttled, four sources still fill the bound, and both
counters stay reachable and testable.

The bound stops being a private literal: `StatesmanDiagnostics.MaxRetainedMaintenanceFailures` and
`StatesmanDiagnostics.MaintenanceFailureRate` are public constants, which closes Phase 15's carried-forward
documentation Minor — the two sentences that say "bounded at 64" as a literal now cite a symbol. It also
closes the Phase 15 final review's first recommended test: with the collection public, the direct read
confirming it holds exactly the newest 64 no longer needs `[InternalsVisibleTo]`, which the repository
still has none of.

Both documentation pages become true. `docs/operations/observability.md:21` currently says "there is
deliberately no API to read or clear that collection"; after this item there is one, and the sentence
says so, with the rate limiter's semantics beside it. `docs/reference/diagnostics.md:27` gains the third
counter.

#### 8. Health checks, and the OpenTelemetry semantic-convention audit

**Where the health check lives, measured.** `Microsoft.Extensions.Diagnostics.HealthChecks.Abstractions`
ships inside the `Microsoft.AspNetCore.App` reference pack for net8.0, net9.0 and net10.0, so
`Statesman.AspNetCore` could implement `IHealthCheck` with no new dependency at all — but a health check
that only exists for ASP.NET Core consumers excludes the worker services that need one most.
`Statesman.Extensions.Hosting` is the right home, and it takes the standalone
`Microsoft.Extensions.Diagnostics.HealthChecks.Abstractions` package reference. Two things were measured
before choosing it. First, **adding a package reference to a baselined package does not trip the
package-validation gate**: `dotnet pack` on `Statesman.Extensions.Hosting` with the reference added and
`PackageValidationBaselineVersion=0.3.0` active succeeded. Second, the gate **was** live in exactly that
configuration — a positive control that added a `bool` parameter to `AddStatesmanHosting` failed with
`CP0002` once per target framework. So the dependency is additive and gated, and no suppression file is
needed. This keeps the package count at twenty-two, which is what
`docs/reference/api-compatibility.md:17` and `:104` state in words.

*Rejected: a new `Statesman.Extensions.Diagnostics.HealthChecks` package.* It would be the tidiest
dependency story and costs a csproj with a blank `PackageValidationBaselineVersion`, a README, a
documentation page, a table-of-contents entry, a solution entry, and a twenty-third package in two
documentation sentences — for one class. *Rejected: `Statesman.AspNetCore`*, for the worker-service
reason above.

**What the health check reports comes from facts that already exist**, never from a new model. Three
inputs: `IStatesman.IsInitialized`; the diagnostics surface item 7 ships (retained maintenance failures
and degraded maintenance stores); and, per root, the loader metadata keys
`docs/reference/diagnostics.md:15-19` already documents. Unhealthy when the runtime is not initialized;
Degraded when maintenance failures are retained or a store is running maintenance without a lease;
Healthy otherwise. The `HealthCheckResult.Data` dictionary carries the counts under the same
`statesman.*` key names the metadata already uses, so a later load-diagnostics bullet adds keys to that
dictionary rather than changing its shape — which is what "designed so it can consume bullet 2 without a
break" means concretely.

`Statesman.Extensions.Hosting` has **no test project today** — `AddStatesmanHosting` and
`StatesmanHostedService` are referenced only by the two samples. This phase adds
`tests/Statesman.Hosting.Tests`, which is the first coverage that package has had and the home for the
health check's tests. It is the phase's one new project, so `eng/validate.py`'s `projects` metric moves
41 to 42 and `Statesman.slnx` gains one entry. It is a test project under `tests/`, so
`src/Directory.Build.props`'s net10-only exclusion list is not involved: `tests/Directory.Build.props`
already pins every test project to a single `net10.0` target.

**The OpenTelemetry audit lands as a documented deviation plus a pinning gate, not as a rename.**
`StatesmanTelemetry` exposes an `ActivitySource` and a `Meter` both named `Statesman`, seven counters
after item 7, one histogram `statesman.operation.duration` with unit `ms`, and four tag keys. Audited
against the OpenTelemetry general and metric conventions, two deviations are real: durations should be
recorded in seconds with unit `s`, and a counter of discrete things should carry an annotation unit such
as `{fault}`. Neither is corrected. Changing the histogram's unit from `ms` to `s` shifts every recorded
value by a factor of a thousand under an unchanged instrument name, which is the worst possible kind of
dashboard break — silent, and wrong in a plausible direction. Adding units to shipped counters changes
the SDK's instrument identity for backends that key on it, for no behavioural gain. Both are recorded
on a new `docs/reference/telemetry.md` with the convention each instrument meets, the two it does not,
why each deviation is kept through 0.x, and what 1.0 will do about them.

What ships instead is the gate: a pinning test in `Statesman.Tests` that asserts the exact name, kind,
unit and tag keys of every instrument on the `Statesman` meter, plus the `ActivitySource` name — the
same treatment Phase 15 gave the public API, for the same reason. A rename or a re-unit then becomes a
deliberate act that fails a named test, rather than a one-line edit nothing notices. Its
break-the-mechanism lever is a scratch rename of one instrument, reverted.

#### 9. The Phase 15 carried-forward sweep

Three items, all small, all named in the Phase 15 handover entry.

- The second of the two test additions the Phase 15 final review recommended: an assertion that
  `RelationalEventId.MigrationAttributeMissingWarning` is logged **exactly once** under repeated
  enumeration, in both `Statesman.EntityFrameworkCore.Tests` and
  `Statesman.Outbox.EntityFrameworkCore.Tests`. Today's assertion is `Assert.Contains`, which a
  once-per-enumeration regression would pass. (The first of the two — the direct read of the retained
  collection — is item 7's, because it needs item 7's surface.)
- Task 4's two documentation sentences that say "bounded at 64" as a literal now cite item 7's public
  constant.
- Task 2's Step 2 methodology note is report-only and needs no change; it is recorded here as closed
  rather than carried a second time.

#### 10. Close-out

`CHANGELOG.md` `[Unreleased]` entries for the conformance suites, the diagnostics surface and its rate
limiter, the third counter, the health check and the telemetry gate; `ROADMAP.md:28` and `:30` annotated
in `:34` and `:36`'s style, and the "0.2 is not complete" paragraph rewritten to say exactly what
remains; a `HANDOVER.md` Phase 16 entry in the Phase 15 entry's shape, with a marked placeholder for the
final review and CI; `docs/toc.yml` gaining `reference/telemetry.md`; and the exit criteria below
amended from predictions to measured values, **every number copied from a task report rather than from a
brief's prediction**.

#### Explicitly parked, with reasons

- **ROADMAP 0.2 bullet 2 — load diagnostics with structured per-source timing and health summaries.
  This is the first Phase 17 item.** It was considered for this phase and does not fit: per-source
  timing means threading a stopwatch through every loader path and deciding whether a timing is
  metadata on a record (which makes it storage) or a side channel (which makes it a new surface), and
  a "health summary" type overlaps the health check item 8 ships. Taking it here would have made the
  phase twelve tasks and would have shipped two health models a month apart. Item 8's health check is
  built so this lands as additional `HealthCheckResult.Data` keys rather than a shape change; nothing
  in this phase needs to be undone to take it.
- **ROADMAP 0.2 bullet 4 — serializer envelopes with content type, serializer id and migration
  provenance.** A storage-format change: stored records carry only a free-form `Metadata` dictionary
  (`Ledger.cs:42`, `:127`), and an envelope changes what every provider writes. Its own design pass.
- **ROADMAP 0.2 bullet 5 — filesystem verify, repair and index-rebuild tooling.** Compaction shipped in
  0.3 (`FileSystemStateLedgerStore.cs:444`); `src/Statesman.Tooling/` holds only export, restore and
  their format and exception types. Parked identically in Phase 15.
- **ROADMAP 0.2 bullet 6 — Redis Cluster coverage and the `HGETALL` to `HashScanAsync` conversion.**
  The script half is covered (`RedisAppendScriptTests.cs`); the cluster half needs a fourth
  infrastructure job, and the scan conversion should ride with it, because a cluster is the environment
  where a server-blocking `HGETALL` matters. Parked identically in Phase 15.
- **ROADMAP 0.2 bullet 8 — analyzer code fixes and stronger collection-mutation data-flow analysis.**
  No `CodeFixProvider` exists anywhere in `src` or `tests`; this is a new surface with its own design
  pass, not a sweep item. Parked identically in Phase 15.
- **A store-format upgrade or restore-from-corruption runbook.** ROADMAP 0.2's "upgrade and corruption
  scenarios are documented" exit criterion stays partial for the same reason Phase 15 gave: corruption
  is documented per provider and upgrade is documented for Entity Framework Core schema migration and
  for declared state migration, but there is no operator runbook. Documentation work with a real design
  question inside it. This phase's conformance suites make the *behaviour* uniform; they do not write
  the runbook.
- **Provider-specific corruption tests are not lifted.** Stated as a park rather than an omission: see
  item 4 for why the shared contract stops at the refusal half.
- **Tiered's `TryGetCapability` reports `IDistributedCapture` true even when its cold tier cannot back
  it.** `TieredStateLedgerStore` declares `IDistributedCapture` on its own type, so the discovery
  method's self-type check (`capabilityType.IsInstanceOfType(this)`) returns true for every Tiered
  instance regardless of what the cold tier actually supports; calling `CaptureAsync` over a
  non-capture-capable cold tier still throws `NotSupportedException`, pinned by
  `TieredDistributedCaptureTests.CaptureAsync_throws_when_the_cold_store_does_not_support_the_capability`.
  This is what made the cancellation suite's Tiered-over-in-memory capability fact genuinely RED at
  baseline during Task 3 (see the amended Exit criteria, item 5, below) rather than the "informative,
  not a defect" row this section's pre-flight measurement expected. Task 3's fix only reordered the
  cancellation check ahead of the capability check; it left the self-type discovery itself untouched.
  Whether Tiered should advertise a capability it cannot always back is a discovery-honesty design
  question for a later phase, not fixed here.
- **`MessageId` collision from ordinal `Root` versus canonical lower-casing.** A 0.4 item, parked
  unchanged in Phases 5, 10, 11, 12, 13 and 15, documented at `docs/providers/index.md:199`. Every fix
  touches *stored* identity and cannot be scoped small.
- **Cross-process filesystem append locking.** A 0.4 item, parked unchanged in Phases 10 through 13 and
  15. A sidecar lock closes the byte-interleaving symptom while two processes still allocate
  overlapping positions, and it re-opens compaction's single-writer design
  (`FileSystemStateLedgerStore.cs:444`).
- **Redis concurrent same-revision `ImportAsync`, beyond the documented sentence.** The real fix is a
  new Lua script on the one write path Phase 8 deliberately left alone.
- **Pipelining the Redis sink**, and **an HTTP webhook sink**. Both parked unchanged; neither is
  observability or conformance.
- **Lifting Redis's `MaxImportablePosition`.** A storage-format change to the Redis feed
  (`RedisStateLedgerStore.cs:93`).
- **A configurable rate limit.** Item 7 ships fixed constants, matching how Phase 15 shipped the 64
  bound. Making the rate configurable means a runtime options object that
  `StatesmanDeclaration.CreateRuntime` does not have today, which is a builder change rather than an
  additive one. The constants are public, so a consumer can at least assert against them.

#### The public API delta

**Additive only. No breaking change, and no capability change.**

New in `Statesman.Abstractions`: `IStatesmanDiagnostics`, `StatesmanDiagnosticsExtensions`,
`MaintenanceFailure`, `MaintenanceFailureDiagnostics`, `StatesmanDiagnostics` (the constants holder).
New in `Statesman`: the third meter instrument, `statesman.maintenance.failures.suppressed`, which is a
metric name rather than a type. New in `Statesman.Extensions.Hosting`: `StatesmanHealthCheck` and an
`AddStatesmanHealthCheck` extension, plus one package reference
(`Microsoft.Extensions.Diagnostics.HealthChecks.Abstractions`), measured not to trip the baseline gate.

*Amended (Task 10, 2026-09-13):* Task 8 measured that `IHealthChecksBuilder` and `AddCheck<T>` live
only in the larger, non-abstractions `Microsoft.Extensions.Diagnostics.HealthChecks` package —
unreachable from the `.Abstractions` package `Statesman.Extensions.Hosting` actually references — so
`StatesmanHealthCheck` shipped **without** an `AddStatesmanHealthCheck` extension. Register it with
`services.AddHealthChecks().AddCheck<StatesmanHealthCheck>("statesman")` instead. The rest of the
delta is as predicted: one new public type, one new package reference, measured not to trip the
baseline gate. (A second `PackageVersion` central entry for the full, non-abstractions
`Microsoft.Extensions.Diagnostics.HealthChecks` package was added for `tests/Statesman.Hosting.Tests`
only, so `AddCheck<StatesmanHealthCheck>` resolves through a real `HealthCheckService` in that test
project — it ships in no `src/` package, so the shipped package count stays 22.)

Nothing changes signature, so no `CompatibilitySuppressions.xml` file is added or edited and
`docs/reference/api-compatibility.md` needs no new entry — only a sentence noting that the seven files
it lists are still seven.

**No capability is added, so `docs/architecture/capabilities.md` gains no row and flips no cell, and
`tests/Statesman.Capabilities.Tests/CapabilityMatrixTests.cs` is untouched.** This is the Phase 10
through 15 precedent, now stated in seven consecutive close-outs. `IStatesmanDiagnostics` is a runtime
surface, not a store capability, and a test pins that it does not extend `IStateCapability` — so the
forwarding policy does not apply to it, and `TieredStateLedgerStore` is not involved.

One new project, `tests/Statesman.Hosting.Tests`, and one new documentation page,
`docs/reference/telemetry.md`.

### Phase 17 — Load diagnostics, honest capability discovery, and the 0.2 sweep (2026-09-13)

Phase 16 shipped ROADMAP 0.2 bullets 1 and 3 and parked bullet 2 — "load diagnostics with structured
per-source timing and health summaries" — as the first Phase 17 item, with the health check shaped so
that load diagnostics would land as additional `HealthCheckResult.Data` keys rather than a shape
change. This phase takes that bullet, sweeps the eleven deferred minors Phase 16 carried forward, and
fixes a 0.3 exit-criterion defect Phase 16 discovered and recorded as parked: `TieredStateLedgerStore`
advertising capabilities its cold tier cannot back.

**What the pre-flight measurement changed about the plan.** Every claim in the controller's brief was
treated as a hypothesis and probed at `97f2753` on a git-clean tree. Seven were wrong, and three of
those changed the design rather than a number:

- **The tiered discovery fix is not where the brief put it, and the brief's version would have shipped
  a change no caller could observe.** `StateCapabilityExtensions.TryGetCapability<TCapability>`
  (`Ledger.cs:591-612`) casts the store to the capability **first** and returns on success. Since
  `TieredStateLedgerStore` declares `IStateChangeFeed`, `IPartitionCatalog`, `IDistributedCapture`,
  `IReplicationLagSource` and `IStateChangeNotifier` on its own type, that cast always wins and the
  `IStateCapabilityProvider` branch below it is dead for exactly the five capabilities in question.
  Every conformance suite and every shipped consumer reaches discovery through that extension. The fix
  is therefore two coordinated edits: a store that declares itself an `IStateCapabilityProvider` becomes
  authoritative for its own discovery, and Tiered's self-type answer consults the tier it delegates to.
- **`docs/architecture/capabilities.md`'s table and `tests/Statesman.Capabilities.Tests/` do not change,
  contrary to the brief's expectation.** `CapabilityMatrixTests.cs:60` compares each documented cell
  against `storeType.GetInterfaces().Contains(capabilityType)` — type reflection, never runtime
  discovery. Tiered still implements all five interfaces after the fix, so every cell and the whole test
  file stay byte-identical; only the prose paragraph below the table changes, and the test matches rows
  by their first cell and never reads it. Measured: with the fix applied as a scratch probe,
  `Statesman.Capabilities.Tests` reported `total: 3`, `failed: 0`. The Phase 10 through 16 "untouched"
  precedent survives into Phase 17.
- **Per-source timing does not belong on the record.** The brief's lean named "per-source elapsed
  milliseconds" among a "small, bounded set of reserved metadata keys", which is a contradiction: a
  per-source key is unbounded in the number of declared sources. Measured, `StateRecord.Metadata` lands
  as JSON on every provider — a filesystem `head.json` and every `history/<revision>.json`, the Redis
  record blob, the Entity Framework Core `MetadataJson` column on both the head and the history entity —
  so a key is roughly 38 to 40 permanent bytes on the head record *and* on every history record, and no
  provider needs a schema change. The existing `statesman.source.<name>.status` key already pays that
  unbounded term once; adding `.duration` beside it would double it. This phase stores three bounded
  whole-load keys instead and puts the per-source breakdown in the typed report and on a new meter
  instrument.

Two further measurements settled questions the brief left open. `RuntimeDefinition.LoadAsync` already
receives the runtime's `TimeProvider` (`RuntimeDefinition.cs:137-142`, passed by
`StateHandle.cs:313-317`), so the clock needs no plumbing; and adding members to `IStatesmanDiagnostics`
is free against the `0.3.0` baseline, because that interface shipped in Phase 16 **after** the tag —
proved by packing `Statesman.Abstractions` with a probe member added (zero `CP` diagnostics) beside a
positive control that added a member to `IStatesman` and produced `CP0006` once per target framework.

#### 1. The conformance-test sweep

`BrokenStoreConformanceTests.cs` is 511 lines after Phase 16's Tasks 2 through 6 each appended to it. It
splits mechanically — seven self-contained sections of two facts plus their doubles, no file-level
shared helper, fourteen facts staying fourteen across seven files. `ImportRejectionConformanceTests`' inner comment
claims `StateRecord.Validate`'s first numeric guard while its assertion only requires some
`ArgumentException`; the assertion is strengthened to pin `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` with
`ParamName` `Revision`, which was measured green on all four import targets including live Redis, and
which keeps the break-the-mechanism half discriminating because the validation-skipping double throws
nothing at all. `ValidationSkippingStore`'s doc comment, which implies it writes the damaged record
through, is corrected to say it re-derives revision and position via an unconditional append.

#### 2. The runtime diagnostics sweep

`PruneFailingStore` is duplicated three times, in two shapes, and the Hosting copy's doc comment claims
the two assemblies "have no shared test helper" — false, since `tests/Shared/` is the established
mechanism and already carries `PausingTimeProvider` and `EntityFrameworkTestProvider` into
`namespace Statesman.TestHelpers` by `<Compile Include ... Link ...>`. It becomes one shared double.
`StatesmanRuntime.cs:448` increments `_maintenanceFailuresReported` outside the gate that
`ReadMaintenanceFailures` reads under, so it moves inside; there is no deterministic test for a window
of a few instructions, and this phase says so rather than inventing a flaky one. The telemetry
convention gate gains a readable failure message and is extended from observing tag keys on
`statesman.commits` alone to observing them on all six per-state instruments, which
`docs/reference/telemetry.md:33-35` already claims — the doc is true in code and the gate was simply
under-gating it.

#### 3. Honest capability discovery on the tiered store

ROADMAP 0.3's exit criterion is "capability discovery tells callers exactly which guarantee is
available", so a Tiered store answering `IDistributedCapture` true over a cold tier that has none is an
exit-criterion defect rather than a nicety. Discovery becomes: a store that implements
`IStateCapabilityProvider` answers for itself, and Tiered answers `this` for a delegated capability only
when the tier it delegates to can back it — cold for the feed, the catalog, the notifier and capture;
both tiers' `IPartitionCatalog` for replication lag. The `IStateLedgerReplica` veto moves above the
self-type check, which the existing Phase 10 comment already said was required the moment that branch
stopped being unconditional.

Measured blast radius, with both edits applied as a probe: **exactly one test in the repository fails**,
`TieredChangeNotifierTests.SubscribeAsync_throws_naming_the_cold_tier_when_cold_has_no_notifier`, which
is the test pinning the old mechanism — its comment reads "Discovery still succeeds ... and the honest
answer arrives at first use." Rewriting it is the substance of the change. `Statesman.Conformance.Tests`
did not move, at `total: 219` with `66` skips without Redis and `196`/`23` with it;
`Statesman.Tests`, `Statesman.Redis.Tests`, `Statesman.Capabilities.Tests`, `Statesman.Tooling.Tests`,
`Statesman.FileSystem.Tests` and `Statesman.EndToEnd.Tests` all stayed `failed: 0`. `dotnet pack` on
`Statesman.Abstractions` and `Statesman.Persistence.Tiered` produced zero `CP` diagnostics: the API
shape does not move, only behaviour.

#### 4. The load report, and per-source timing off the runtime's clock

`RuntimeDefinition.LoadAsync` times the whole load and each source with
`TimeProvider.GetTimestamp`/`GetElapsedTime` on the runtime's own clock, so a test using
`ManualTimeProvider` — whose `GetTimestamp` and `TimestampFrequency` overrides have made elapsed time
exact since Phase 13 — asserts exact elapsed values rather than real-time bounds. The default
`StateSourceExecution.Sequential` is what the exact assertions run at; the parallel path records the
same timings but two sources advancing one virtual clock interleave, so it gets a shape assertion only.

Three new reserved metadata keys land on the record, bounded and independent of source count:
`statesman.load.duration.ms`, `statesman.load.started` and `statesman.load.completed`, the last two as
round-trip invariant-culture strings. Four new public types in `Statesman.Abstractions` carry the
structured half: `StateSourceLoadReport` (name, status, elapsed, exception type and message when
faulted), `StateLoadReport` (address, started, completed, elapsed, completeness, ready and faulted
counts, the per-source list) and `LoadDiagnostics` (the retained reports), read through a new
`IStatesmanDiagnostics.ReadLoadDiagnostics()`.

A load that throws outright — `RequireAll` with a failure, or no source producing state — records no
report, because it becomes a fault snapshot instead, which `statesman.faults` and the snapshot's own
`StateError` already surface. A `partial` or `initial-fallback` load completes and does record one.

#### 5. The load-diagnostics surface, bounded

Reports are retained latest-per-address in the runtime, bounded at
`StatesmanDiagnostics.MaxRetainedLoadReports` = 64, matching how the maintenance-failure queue is
bounded — a partitioned state's address space is unbounded, so an unbounded map keyed by address is the
same leak Phase 15 closed for maintenance failures. Eviction is oldest-completed first.

#### 6. One new instrument

`statesman.load.source.duration`, a `Histogram<double>` with unit `ms`, carrying the four documented tag
keys plus a fifth, `statesman.source`. Milliseconds rather than seconds deliberately, matching
`statesman.operation.duration` rather than introducing a second unit convention inside one meter — both
move together at 1.0 under the deviation decision 60 already records. No shipped instrument is renamed
or re-united. `docs/reference/telemetry.md` and the pinning gate are updated in the same task.

#### 7. The health summary, without a second health model

Decision 61 rejected a `HealthSummary` type to avoid two health models, and this phase honours that: the
typed load report **is** the model, and `StatesmanHealthCheck` derives five new `Data` keys from it —
`statesman.load.reports`, `statesman.load.reports.incomplete`, `statesman.load.sources.faulted`,
`statesman.load.slowest.source` and `statesman.load.slowest.duration.ms`. Decision 61's amendment
predicted exactly this: keys added to the same dictionary, no shape change.

The status rule is decided rather than left open. **A retained report whose completeness is `partial` or
`initial-fallback` makes the check Degraded**, joining "maintenance failures retained" and "a store
maintaining without a lease". It is not Unhealthy, because the state is usable and authoritative; and it
self-corrects, because reports are latest-per-address and the next complete refresh of that address
replaces the degraded one. `complete`, `seeded` and `retained` are healthy.

#### 8. Record metadata round-trips on every provider

No shared conformance fact asserts that `StateRecord.Metadata` survives a provider round trip at all
today. One does now, across all five providers: the dictionary survives append, `ReadLatestAsync`,
`ReadHistoryAsync`, and an export-restore cycle, with the reserved `statesman.load.*` key shapes as its
data. Reserved keys already survive export and restore by construction — `StateLedgerExportFormat.cs:152`
and `:174` copy `record.Metadata` wholesale with no prefix filter — and this pins it.
