   |---|---|
   | STM001's new indexer skip disabled | **FIRES**, `failed: 4` — all four rows of `A_collection_indexer_assignment_is_STM004s_alone_and_never_also_STM001` |
   | the walk's ignored-**owner** stop restored to "continue outward" | **FIRES**, `failed: 2` — `s.Ig.Value = 1;` and `s.Ig.Items.Add(1);` |
   | the walk's ignored-**member** stop restored to "continue outward" | **FIRES**, `failed: 1` — `s.Child.Hidden.Value = 1;` |
   | the walk's `MemberBindingExpressionSyntax` arm disabled | **FIRES**, `failed: 1` — `s.Plain?.Items.Add(1);` |
   | STM004's message rebuilt from `access.Expression` | **FIRES**, `failed: 1` — `Actual: "'.Items.Add' mutates …"` |
   | STM001's `IsInitialization` guard disabled | **FIRES**, `failed: 3` — all three initializer rows |
   | `IsInitialization`'s `ObjectCreationExpressionSyntax` arm alone | **FIRES**, `failed: 1` — `var b = new Bag { Scalar = 1 };` |
   | `IsInitialization`'s `ImplicitObjectCreationExpressionSyntax` arm alone | **FIRES**, `failed: 1` — `Bag b = new() { Scalar = 1 };` |
   | `IsInitialization`'s `WithExpressionSyntax` arm alone | **FIRES**, `failed: 1` — `r = r with { Value = 1 };` |
   | `IsAllowedBoundary`'s `IgnoreAttribute` operand deleted | **FIRES**, `failed: 1` — `An_ignored_enclosing_method_is_silent_for_both_rules` |
   | the walk's `ElementAccessExpressionSyntax` step disabled | **FIRES**, `failed: 1` — `s.Plains[0].Value = 1;` |
   | the walk's `ParenthesizedExpressionSyntax` step disabled | **FIRES**, `failed: 1` — `(s.Plain.Items)[0] = 1;`, **not** the fact the review named (see below) |
   | the walk's `ConditionalAccessExpressionSyntax` step disabled | **FIRES**, `failed: 1` — `(s.Plain?.Items)[0] = 1;` |
   | STM004's `IsCollectionInterface(type)` operand deleted | **FIRES**, `failed: 1` — `s.Coll.Add(1);` |
   | STM004's indexer `property.SetMethod is not null` operand deleted | **FIRES**, `failed: 1` — `s.Refs[0] = 1;` |
   | `IsCollectionInterface`'s `System.Collections.Generic` operand deleted | **FIRES**, `failed: 1` — `s.Decoyed.Add(1);` |
   | the code fix's new `volatile` guard disabled | **FIRES**, `failed: 1` — fixed source `public volatile readonly int Value;` |

   **One named fact did not discriminate, and was replaced rather than reported as covered.** The review
   named `(s.Items).Add(1)` as the fact for the walk's `ParenthesizedExpressionSyntax` step. It does not
   discriminate: `GetSymbolInfo` on a parenthesized expression answers with the inner expression's
   symbol, so the walk finds `Items` on `Bag` at the first link and never steps at all. With the step
   disabled that fact still passes. `(s.Plain.Items)[0] = 1;` does discriminate, because the parenthesis
   has to be entered before `s.Plain` can be reached; the same reasoning gives
   `(s.Plain?.Items)[0] = 1;` for the `ConditionalAccessExpressionSyntax` step, which is the one shape
   that puts a conditional access on the chain — a null-conditional invocation hands the walk a member
   binding instead. Both facts were added and both levers fire. `(s.Items).Add(1)` is kept as a
   non-discriminating behaviour fact, labelled as one in the source.

   **One further review prediction was wrong, in the fix wave's favour.** Finding I4 asked for
   confirmation that STM001 on `s?.Plain.Value = 1` is a compile error, `CS0131`. It is not: C# 14's
   null-conditional assignment makes that shape legal, measured as zero compiler errors on this language
   version, and all three probed variants (`s?.Plain.Value = 1`, `s?.Scalar = 1`, `s.Child?.Value = 1`)
   report STM001 correctly. A fact was added for the first rather than a note that the shape cannot
   occur. Its owner is reached without the new walk arm, because the binding `.Plain` is itself declared
   on the managed `Bag`; the arm is what carries the harder shape, `s.Plain?.Items.Add(1)`.
7. A docfx build of `docs/docfx.json` confirms all four `helpLinkUri` anchors exist in the generated
   `docs/_site/guides/analyzers.html`, and introduces no documentation warning beyond the eleven
   `InvalidFileLink` warnings present at `017e92a`.

   *Amended (Task 8, 2026-09-14):* The four anchors measured present, exactly as Task 6 recorded:
   `stm001-direct-managed-state-mutation`, `stm002-publicly-mutable-managed-state-shape`,
   `stm003-dynamic-state-key`, `stm004-managed-state-collection-mutation`. The warning count did **not**
   hold at eleven: a docfx build at this close-out's own final commit measures **13** `InvalidFileLink`
   warnings, all still the same pre-existing kind (a relative link from `ROADMAP.md`, which sits outside
   `docfx.json`'s content root, into `docs/`). The two new ones are this close-out's own required edits:
   `ROADMAP.md`'s bullet 8 now links the phrase "the 0.3 design spec" to this document the same way every
   other shipped 0.2 bullet already does (bullet 5 alone already contributed two such warnings, one at its
   own bullet and one where the closing recount paragraph mentions it again), and the closing recount's new
   "Bullet 8 … now ships" sentence links the same target a second time in the same paragraph, matching that
   same established pattern rather than inventing a new one. Both are the identical, already-accepted
   `InvalidFileLink` class the other seven ROADMAP.md instances are, at two new line numbers; no other
   warning class appears and `0 error(s)` holds. This is a measured deviation from the plan's prediction:
   declaring an eighth 0.2 bullet shipped, in the same style every previously-shipped bullet uses, was
   always going to add to this count, and the plan's own "eleven, present at `017e92a`" prediction did not
   anticipate that its own close-out step would be the one adding to it.
8. `CHANGELOG.md` carries a `### Changed` entry for STM001's widened reach, because it is a behaviour change
   to a shipped rule, and a `### Fixed` entry for the Redis dispose race.

   *Amended (Task 8, 2026-09-14):* Both present. The `### Changed` entry for STM001's receiver-chain
   ownership walk was written by Task 6. The `### Fixed` entry for the Redis dispose race was owed and
   unwritten before this close-out; it is added here, describing the final, fix-round-corrected guard
   shape — the SUBSCRIBE guard requires `linked.IsCancellationRequested` for all five caught types, and the
   read loop's guard requires it for four of the five, because `OperationCanceledException` alone is already
   conclusive there under `WithCancellation(linked.Token)` — rather than the plan's originally predicted
   single symmetric guard shape, which Task 7's review found let a genuine non-disposal failure escape as a
   silent clean end.

#### Explicitly parked, with reasons

- **B1-06, alias tracking for STM004** (`var items = state.Items; items.Add(1)`). Option (i) has no data
  flow by design. Closing it is option (ii): find the local's single initializer, re-run the receiver walk on
  it, and bail if the local is ever reassigned — plus a fact each for the `out`, `ref` and lambda-capture
  corners. Sized **M** on its own, and documented in the analyzer guide's limitations sentence as the one
  shape the rule looks like it should catch and does not.
- **Option (iii), full data flow for STM004.** Aliases through fields, through calls, and collections of
  collections would need `DataFlowAnalysis`/`ControlFlowGraph` per method body, and the false-positive
  surface grows with every path. Rejected for 0.2.
- **An STM003 code fix.** Constant folding already silences every foldable argument shape — literal,
  literal concatenation, `const` field, `nameof`, fully-constant interpolation — so the only mechanically
  fixable row left is `static readonly string` to `const string`, which has **zero** occurrences in this
  repository, edits a different member from the one the diagnostic points at, possibly in a different file,
  and is correct only when that field's initializer is itself constant and nothing else assigns it. It would
  ship with no complainant and no in-repo signal.
- **A `SymbolFinder`-gated `readonly` field fix**, offering the arm only when every write is inside the
  declaring type's constructor. Not prototyped. The arm ships as-is instead: Roslyn previews a code action
  before it applies, `CS0191` names the offending write precisely, and that write was already an STM001
  warning. The consequence is documented rather than engineered around.
- **An STM002 companion shape rule for a `List<T>`-typed managed member.** Zero occurrences on any
  `[ManagedState]` type in this repository, so it would ship with no signal, and being enabled by default it
  would newly warn every consumer who chose a mutable collection deliberately behind a boundary. Sized **S**
  on top of STM002's existing `AnalyzeShape` loop if it is ever wanted, and it would need its own id for the
  same reasons STM004 does.
- **A conformance fact for the Redis dispose race.** The defect is Redis-specific in its cause — a
  multiplexer the store owns, and a client library whose `SubscribeAsync` takes no token — and
  `ChangeNotifierConformanceTests` already asserts the right thing for every provider. A shared fact would
  have to invent a socket-stall mechanism for four providers that have no socket.
- **Pinning the `IOException`/`SocketException` half of the Redis catch set.** ~~Measured not to be
  discriminated by the stalled fact.~~ *Amended (fix wave, 2026-09-14): no longer parked and no longer a
  gap. Over twenty consecutive runs, Lever 12 (both types removed from both guards) failed the existing
  stalled fact 3 times, with the `IOException`-wrapping-`SocketException` shape thrown from the SUBSCRIBE
  await. `StallProxy` does reproduce that shape, intermittently rather than on demand, so the fact
  discriminates both halves of the catch set and no second stall mode is needed. See criterion 6's fix-wave
  amendment for the measurement.* A **deterministic** reproduction of that shape would still need a second
  stall mode that aborts the socket rather than swallowing writes; that, and only that, stays parked, and
  it now buys determinism rather than coverage.
- **A discriminating test for the read loop's widened-type operand.** Task 7's fix round added
  `linked.IsCancellationRequested` for the four widened types in the read loop's guard, and the
  supplementary lever that removes it fires on nothing — confirmed 5/5 by the fix round and 5/5 again by
  the final review. The fact written for it,
  `A_multiplexer_disposed_by_its_actual_owner_must_not_end_the_read_loop_with_a_silent_false`, could not
  fail either: its only assertion sat behind `pending.IsCompletedSuccessfully`, and neither measured
  scenario completes `pending` at all, because StackExchange.Redis's `ChannelMessageQueue` neither throws
  nor completes on a bare `Dispose` of a multiplexer the store does not own. The fix wave deleted it
  (final-review minor M3) rather than leave a green fact standing as coverage. **The operand ships with
  zero test coverage, and that is recorded here rather than implied to be covered.** The shape that would
  close it is a unit-level fact that injects the exception at the enumerator — not a third `StallProxy`
  mode, which the measurements above show cannot reach the read loop at all. Sized **S**, not attempted.
- **STM004 and STM001 on an `event` add or remove.** `s.Changed += (a, b) => { };` on a `[ManagedState]`
  type is silent, because an `IEventSymbol` is neither an `IPropertySymbol` nor an `IFieldSymbol` and
  `ManagedStateMutationAnalyzer.AnalyzeMutation` tests for those two. Pre-existing, not introduced by this
  phase, and arguably not a state mutation at all: subscribing to an event does not change the state
  value. Recorded from the final review's probe (minor M7) so the next reader finds it measured rather
  than guessed.
- **Extracting the shared `GetSyntaxRootAsync` preamble from `MakeInitOnlyAsync` and `MakeReadOnlyAsync`**
  (Task 3 minor M-a). Three lines, brief-verbatim, and the logic after the root genuinely differs —
  accessor replacement versus modifier insertion. The reviewer explicitly did not ask for a fix, and the
  final review endorsed parking it: extracting it would cost more clarity than it buys.
- **A second fix-all fact for the `readonly` field arm** (Task 3 minor M-b). Fix-all is proven for the
  property arm only. The mechanism under test is the shared `equivalenceKey`, which is the *same*
  mechanism for both arms and is pinned by its own lever, so a second fix-all fact would re-prove one
  mechanism. Parked with that reason by the final review.
- **`StallProxy`'s orphaned pump task after `Task.WhenAny`, and `DisposeAsync` not awaiting the
  per-connection `ProxyAsync` tasks** (Task 7 minors M-a and M-b). Both are in a test-only helper,
  fire-and-forget by construction, bounded by `_shutdown`, cancelled by `DisposeAsync`, and the resulting
  exception is swallowed deliberately. No leak beyond the test process. Parked by the final review.
- **ROADMAP 0.2 bullet 4 — serializer envelopes.** Unchanged from Phases 15 through 19: a storage-format
  change with its own design pass, and the only remaining 0.2 bullet that can break a shipped store on disk.
  `IStateSerializer` has no notion of content type or serializer identity, and adding one means either three
  new `StateRecord` members or a framing prefix on `Payload`, touching the filesystem `FileRecord` JSON,
  Redis's member-exact opaque bytes across two keys, the Entity Framework Core columns plus a migration for
  three engines, and `Statesman.Tooling`'s export/import format. Sized **L**, not measured.
- **ROADMAP 0.2 bullet 6 — Redis Cluster coverage.** Unchanged from Phase 19: the CI job is the small half,
  and the **key layout is the large half**. Two `ITransaction` sites deliberately span hash slots and are
  documented standalone-only, two Lua scripts take `KEYS` spanning four different keys, and six key builders
  would have to be wrapped in a hash tag. That is a design change to the stored key shape and needs its own
  ruling first (decision 99). Sized **L**, not measured.

  *Amended (Phase 21, 2026-09-14):* every count in this bullet is wrong, and each is wrong in the
  direction that understates the work. There are **seven** key builders in
  `Statesman.Persistence.Redis` (`HeadKey`, `RevisionKey`, `HistoryKey`, `GlobalPositionKey`,
  `ChangeFeedKey`, `PartitionsKey`, `LeaseKey`, with `StreamKey` feeding the first three) plus two in
  `Statesman.Outbox.Redis`; **three** `CreateTransaction()` sites, at `RedisStateLedgerStore.cs:315`,
  `:463` and `:791`, of which `PruneAsync` at `:463` was never named; and **five** Lua scripts across
  the two projects, of which `AppendScript` alone spans **six** keys. Phase 21 measured the layout
  rather than sizing it: see its own section.
- **Changing `MaxBytes`'s `continue` to a `break`.** Unchanged from Phase 19 decision 90.
- **Lifting verify and repair into the shared conformance suite.** Unchanged from Phase 16 decision 63 and
  Phases 17 through 19: the other four providers have no textual structure a crash can leave half-written.
- **A cross-process filesystem append lock**, and with it multi-process writers. Unchanged from Phases 11
  through 19.
- **A persisted change-log index**, **repairing a `MisplacedStreamDirectory` by moving it**, **deleting an
  `OrphanedTemporaryFile`**, and **rewriting a `MalformedChangeLogLine`.** All unchanged from Phase 18.
- **A polling `ExpireAsync`** and **a server-side lease lever (`PEXPIRE key 1`)**. Unchanged from Phase 19
  decision 93.
- **Refactoring the three `OrphanedTemporaryFile` loops in `FileSystemLedgerRecovery.cs`.** Closed by
  measurement in Phase 19 decision 96, not carried.
- **`MessageId` collision from ordinal `Root` versus canonical lower-casing**, **Redis concurrent
  same-revision `ImportAsync` beyond the documented sentence**, **pipelining the Redis sink**, **an HTTP
  webhook sink**, and **lifting Redis's `MaxImportablePosition`.** All 0.4 or storage-format items, each
  re-ruled at least seven times.
- **What this phase's pre-flight did not measure**, carried forward as caveats rather than as facts:
  whether `redis:7-alpine` can host a single-node cluster in a GitHub Actions job (carried from Phase 19,
  still unmeasured); whether the declined `AddOrphanedTemporaries` helper compiles — the eight-line figure is
  a line count, not a build (carried from Phase 19, still unmeasured); the Redis guard's behaviour on Linux
  and macOS, where `SocketException` 995 does not exist and CI's `build-test` job is the first measurement;
  STM004's compile-time cost, since three more `RegisterSyntaxNodeAction` registrations were added, one of
  them on every `InvocationExpression`, with no build-time measurement taken; and STM004 against real
  consumer code beyond this repository's own sample. The Phase 18 minor M-b process note is **not** carried:
  it is closed by this phase's close-out as item 8 of `docs/superpowers/HANDOVER.md`'s "How this effort
  works" list, which is where a permanent dispatch convention belongs.

#### The public API delta

**Additive, two public types, in a package whose validation gate cannot see either of them.**

- `Statesman.Analyzers.ManagedStateCollectionMutationAnalyzer`, a `DiagnosticAnalyzer`, in the existing
  `Statesman.Analyzers` assembly.
- `Statesman.CodeFixes.MutableShapeCodeFixProvider`, a `CodeFixProvider`, in the new `Statesman.CodeFixes`
  assembly.

Nothing is removed and nothing changes signature. `DiagnosticDescriptors` stays `internal`, so STM004's
descriptor is **not** public surface — the research prototype declared it as a public static field on the
analyzer class, and that is deliberately not what ships, because the three existing descriptors are internal
and a public descriptor would be a new API commitment for no consumer.

Neither type moves APICompat, for two independent reasons measured in the pre-flight: they land in
`analyzers/dotnet/cs`, which package validation does not inspect, and the gate compares nothing for this
package anyway, since neither the `0.3.0` baseline nupkg nor the new one has any `lib/` or `ref/` asset. The
lever proved it: removing a public type from the baselined package produced no `CP0002`. **No
`CompatibilitySuppressions.xml` is added or edited.**

`Statesman.Persistence.Redis` changes behaviour without changing surface: two `catch` clauses inside an
existing method body. **No capability is added, so `docs/architecture/capabilities.md` gains no row and flips
no cell, and `tests/Statesman.Capabilities.Tests/CapabilityMatrixTests.cs` is untouched.**
`docs/reference/public-api.md` is untouched, because it is a curated runtime-API map that lists no packages
and no analyzer ids.

### Phase 21 — STM004 completeness, the ownership walk, and Redis Cluster (2026-09-14)

Phase 20's close-out carried two analyzer items forward as Phase 21 candidates and left ROADMAP 0.2
bullet 6 where Phase 16 had left it: "the CI job is the small half, and the key layout is the large
half", sized **L** and never measured. The pre-flight research for this phase
(`.superpowers/sdd/2026-09-14-phase-21-research/report.md`, 914 lines, every number measured at
`c9cec46`) measured all of it, and two of its findings were on nobody's list. A **cast breaks the
ownership walk for both rules** — `((List<int>)state.Coll).Add(1)` and
`((PlainBag)state.Plain).Value = 1` are silent — an entire false-negative class Phase 20's
nineteen-lever sweep could not find, because that sweep pinned operands that exist rather than arms
that were never written. And the spec's own key-layout sentence is **wrong on all three of its
counts**, each in the direction that understates the work.

The research also answered bullet 6. A per-store hash tag takes `Statesman.Conformance.Tests` from
**47 failures to zero** against a real single-node `redis:7-alpine` cluster, identical to its
standalone result, and `Statesman.Outbox.Redis` already passes on a cluster unchanged because every
one of its operations is single-key. What remained was a product decision about whether keys may
move, and a CI job. This phase takes both: the layout ships as an **opt-in option with a `Legacy`
default**, so no existing deployment's data moves, and a `redis-cluster` job runs the four
Redis-affected projects against a cluster with that option selected.

Every prototype the research left behind was re-measured against `98bf54f` while this plan was
written, in a scratch worktree, after the dependency refresh moved StackExchange.Redis to 3.2.1 —
including the one piece of the research that was never prototyped at all (the walk's conversion
arms) and the one that depends on a third party's internals (the read-loop seam).

#### 1. Dependabot stops proposing Roslyn

`.github/dependabot.yml` gains an `ignore:` entry for `Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.Common`,
`Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp` and `Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp.Workspaces`, with the reason in a
YAML comment beside it: **the Roslyn version `Statesman.Analyzers` references is its consumers'
compiler floor.** A consumer whose SDK ships an older Roslyn cannot load an analyzer built against a
newer one, so that pin moves by hand together with `global.json`'s SDK pin and never on a bot's
schedule. The dependency refresh measured exactly this and closed two Dependabot pull requests for
it, `#12` and `#13`, both proposing 5.9.0 as a `VersionOverride` on six migration projects — a shape
`eng/validate.py` rejects outright, and a bump nothing in the restore graph forces.

The three names are exact rather than wildcards, so `Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp.CodeFix.Testing`,
a test-only pin with no consumer-facing floor, keeps receiving updates through the `dotnet-platform`
group. `eng/validate.py` does read this file — it parses it as YAML, rejects tab characters, and
requires the literal tokens `groups:` and `update-types:` to survive — all three of which the entry
preserves; measured PASS.

#### 2. STM004's membership rule and its mutator set

The Phase 20 minor said `IsInPlaceCollectionMember` tests only `ICollection<T>`, leaving `Queue<T>`,
`Stack<T>` and the non-generic collections unflagged. That is true and materially incomplete. A probe
over 48 members of one `[ManagedState]` type, covering 85 non-alias shapes that all compile with zero
compiler errors, measured **36 firing, 41 silent gaps and 8 correctly silent** at `c9cec46`. The gaps
fall into three groups, and the third is the one the minor did not name:

| Group | Shapes | Cause |
|---|---|---|
| Non-generic-only collections | `Queue<T>`, `Stack<T>`, `ArrayList`, `Hashtable`, `BitArray`, `IList`, `IDictionary`, `StringCollection`, `NameValueCollection`, the legacy `Queue`/`Stack` | they implement `System.Collections.ICollection`, never `ICollection<T>` |
| Concurrent collections | `ConcurrentQueue<T>`, `ConcurrentStack<T>`, `ConcurrentBag<T>`, `BlockingCollection<T>`, `IProducerConsumerCollection<T>` | same cause: `IProducerConsumerCollection<T>` extends the **non-generic** `ICollection` |
| Names missing on types that already qualify | `SortedSet<T>.RemoveWhere`, `ObservableCollection<T>.Move`, `LinkedList<T>.AddFirst`/`AddLast`/`RemoveFirst`, `ConcurrentDictionary<,>.GetOrAdd`/`AddOrUpdate`/`TryRemove` | the declaring type passes; the **name** is not in `Mutators` |

`ObservableCollection<T>.Add` fires while `.Move` on the same member is silent;
`ConcurrentDictionary<,>.TryAdd` fires while `.AddOrUpdate` on the same member is silent. The guide
reads as if the boundary were the type. It is partly the name, and that reads as a bug rather than a
scope limit.

The membership rule becomes one principled test, interface-based, with no type whitelist anywhere:

> A type mutates in place when it implements (or is) `System.Collections.Generic.ICollection<T>`
> **or** `System.Collections.ICollection`, and is not a top-level type in
> `System.Collections.Immutable`.

Both operands are load-bearing and neither implies the other. The non-generic operand admits
`Queue<T>`, `Stack<T>`, every `IProducerConsumerCollection<T>`, `BlockingCollection<T>` and every
legacy collection, because `IList`, `IDictionary` and `IProducerConsumerCollection<T>` all extend it.
The generic operand stays because `ISet<T>`, `IList<T>`, `IDictionary<,>` and `ICollection<T>` do
**not** extend the non-generic one, so an interface-typed member is reached by nothing else. The
existing `ContainingType is null` immutable guard is untouched. `Mutators` gains twenty-six names,
from sixteen to forty-two.

Two names in that set need a stated reason, because both look unsafe and are not. `Take` is also
`Enumerable.Take`, but LINQ's declaring type is `System.Linq.Enumerable`, which is no collection, so
the type test guards it — pinned by a fact, `_ = state.Items.Take(2);`, which must stay silent.
`GetOrAdd` mutates only conditionally; it is in the set because a write attempt against managed state
is what STM004 exists to name.

**Measured at `98bf54f`.** Fifteen new `Mutating` rows and three new `Silent` rows;
`Statesman.Analyzers.Tests` rises from `total: 77` to `total: 95`, `failed: 0`. `dotnet build
Statesman.slnx` stays `45 projects, 0 errors, 0 warnings` with `samples/Statesman.Sample.Migration`'s
`obj` cleared, and the pass is non-vacuous: adding a `Queue<string>` member to that sample's
`[ManagedState]` record and calling `Enqueue` produces
`error STM004: 'probe.Audit.Enqueue' mutates the collection held by managed state 'SessionState' …`
at `Program.cs(18,1)`. The research additionally measured the prototype against a five-project build
with the analyzer wired into `Statesman.Sample.AspNetCore`, `Statesman.Sample.Console`,
`Statesman.Tests` and `Statesman.EndToEnd.Tests`: **zero new warnings, zero false positives**, which
is a stronger check than the repository's own single-sample gate.

#### 3. The ownership walk's conversion arms

Research finding D2, recorded nowhere in the spec, the handover or the guide, and **the one part of
this phase the research never prototyped**. `ManagedStateOwnership.ResolveManagedStateOwner` has arms
for member access, element access, conditional access, parenthesis and member binding, and none for a
cast, an `as` expression or the null-forgiving operator. A conversion is not a link in the chain, it
is a lens on one, and the walk stopped at it:

| Case | At `98bf54f` | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| `((List<int>)s.Coll).Add(1);` | silent | STM004 |
| `((ICollection<int>)s.Readonlies).Add(1);` | silent | STM004 |
| `(s.Coll as List<int>).Add(1);` | silent | STM004 |
| `((PlainBag)s.Plain).Value = 1;` | silent | **STM001** |

Three `switch` arms close it. The measurement that mattered, and that the research's own proposed
test rows would have got wrong: **`s.Plain!.Value = 1` and `s.Plain!.Items.Add(1)` do not need the
null-forgiving arm at all.** Roslyn's `GetSymbolInfo` on `s.Plain!` answers with `Plain`'s own symbol,
so the walk finds the owner at that link and never steps. The arm is reached only when the suppressed
expression has no symbol of its own — `((PlainBag)s.Plain)!.Value = 1` and
`(s.Coll as List<int>)!.Add(1)` — and those are the two rows that ship, because those are the two that
discriminate.

**Measured at `98bf54f`.** `Statesman.Analyzers.Tests` rises from `total: 95` to `total: 102`,
`failed: 0`: four new STM004 rows and three new STM001 rows. One lever per arm, each applied alone:
removing the cast arm fails 4, removing the `as` arm fails 3, removing the null-forgiving arm fails 2.

*Amended (Phase 21 fix round, 2026-09-14):* the cast arm above is narrower than this section states.
Task 3's fix round found that a user-defined conversion operator, implicit or explicit, invokes a static method that
can return a different object, so it stops the walk: `((Exported)s.Plain).Value = 1`, where `PlainBag`
declares `public static explicit operator Exported(PlainBag)`, stays silent by design, detected as
`GetSymbolInfo(cast).Symbol is IMethodSymbol { MethodKind: MethodKind.Conversion }`. An ordinary
reference, boxing, unboxing or numeric cast keeps walking; a BCL `decimal` conversion is also
`MethodKind.Conversion` and stops the walk too, unobservable for both rules today. The `as` and
null-forgiving arms are unaffected: the language forbids a user-defined conversion in `as`, and the
suppression arm is purely syntactic. The count above is also stale: the fix round added two more
tests (the `Exported` case for both rules), taking `Statesman.Analyzers.Tests` to `total: 104` before
Task 5 began.

#### 4. The Redis read loop's widened-type operand

Phase 20's parked list records this operand as shipping "with zero test coverage … Sized **S**, not
attempted", and names the shape that would close it: "a unit-level fact that injects the exception at
the enumerator — not a third `StallProxy` mode". That is exactly what ships.

The seam is entirely test-side. **No production change of any kind, and no `[InternalsVisibleTo]`** —
the repository's zero-`InternalsVisibleTo` count is a deliberate, preserved convention. The store's
constructor already takes an `IConnectionMultiplexer`, so a `System.Reflection.DispatchProxy` stands
in for the multiplexer and the subscriber in about forty lines, where hand-rolling the two interfaces
would need roughly ninety stub members. The one concrete type on the path is StackExchange.Redis's
sealed `ChannelMessageQueue`, reached through its internal constructor and faulted through its
internal `MarkCompleted(Exception)`.

Two risks were measured rather than assumed. The exception surfaces from `MoveNextAsync`
**unwrapped**, not as a `ChannelClosedException`. And the internals the reflection depends on are
**unchanged at StackExchange.Redis 3.2.1**, the version `98bf54f` moved to after the research ran:
one non-public instance constructor taking `(ref RedisChannel, RedisSubscriber)`, one
`Void MarkCompleted(System.Exception)`, and the `_queue` field the readiness probe reads.

That readiness probe is what makes the fact deterministic rather than a sleep: the store has reached
`MoveNextAsync` exactly when the underlying channel has a blocked reader.

**Measured at `98bf54f`.** One new file, one four-row theory, no live Redis so it never skips.
`Statesman.Redis.Tests` rises from `total: 44` to `total: 48`, `failed: 0`, and at defaults with
`STATESMAN_TEST_REDIS` unset the four new rows **succeed** rather than skip. The lever — deleting
`&& linked.IsCancellationRequested` from `RedisStateLedgerStore.cs:655` — fires `failed: 4`: all four
rows become a clean end.

#### 5. STM004 and STM001 gain one hop of alias tracking

Design option (ii), parked since Phase 20 as B1-06 and sized **M** on reasoning. Measured, the code is
one method on `ManagedStateOwnership` reached from a new `IdentifierNameSyntax` arm of the walk: it
returns the local's sole initializer so the walk continues through it, or `null`, which is today's
behaviour for every local. It lands in the shared walk, so **both** rules gain it — `var p = s.Plain;
p.Value = 1;` becomes an STM001 report — which makes it a second behaviour change to a shipped rule
in this phase, with its own `### Changed` changelog entry.

The bail-outs are what keep it from becoming a false-positive surface, and this phase ships **four**,
not the research prototype's eight:

- the identifier does not resolve to an `ILocalSymbol`;
- the local has more than one declaring syntax reference, or its declaring syntax is not a
  `VariableDeclaratorSyntax` with an initializer — one check that rejects `foreach` variables,
  pattern variables and deconstruction designations, none of which are declarators;
- any other reference to the local is the left side of an assignment;
- any other reference is a `ref`/`out`/`in` argument;
- any other reference is the operand of `++` or `--`.

**The prototype's lambda and local-function bail-out is dropped, measured as redundant.** It bailed
whenever any reference sat inside a lambda declared outside the local's own scope. Every rebind that
could happen there is *already* an assignment or an `out`/`ref` argument, so the two checks above
catch it — pinned by a fact, `var m = s.Items; Capture(() => m = new List<int>()); m.Add(1);`, which
is silent and whose lever is the assignment check. What the dropped bail-out uniquely suppressed was a
lambda that only **reads** the local, which defers the mutation without changing which object is
mutated: `var e = s.Items; Capture(() => e.Add(1));` is a true positive and now reports.

The prototype's unary check is also narrowed, for a measured reason: a postfix `!` is a
`PostfixUnaryExpressionSyntax` too, so testing the node type alone would have made every
null-forgiving mention of an alias a bail-out. The shipped check tests the four increment and
decrement kinds by `SyntaxKind`.

**Measured at `98bf54f`.** `Statesman.Analyzers.Tests` rises from `total: 102` to `total: 113`,
`failed: 0`: five new `Mutating` rows, five new `Silent` rows, and the existing
`var l = s.Items; l.Add(1);` row relocated from `Silent` to `Mutating` — that row existed only to pin
the gap as deliberate, so it moves rather than being deleted. Four levers, one per bail-out, each
applied alone: the assignment check fails 2, the `ref`/`out` check fails 1, the increment check fails
1, and loosening the declarator check to accept a `foreach` variable fails 1. The last of those fires
on the `foreach` row alone; the deconstruction and pattern rows have no initializer syntax to
substitute at all, so one check silences all three and only one of them can be made to report. Said
here rather than implied.

*Amended (Phase 21 fix round, 2026-09-14):* two corrections from Task 5's fix round. First, the walk
does not stop after one hop: `SingleInitializerOf`'s result is re-dispatched through the same switch,
so a chain of aliases resolves fully — `var a = s.Items; var b = a; b.Add(1);` reports — one hop per
identifier, applied each time the walk lands on an identifier, not a single hop total. Second, a fifth
bail-out shipped after this section was written: a deconstructing assignment into an existing local,
`(x, _) = (new List<int>(), 0);`, is a rebind, caught by climbing `x`'s ancestor chain through any run
of `ArgumentSyntax`/`TupleExpressionSyntax`/`ParenthesizedExpressionSyntax` wrappers to the enclosing
assignment's `Left`. A third, unrelated defect was found and fixed in the same round: the walk now
carries a visited-node set so a self-referential declarator (`List<int> x = x;`) cannot hang it.
`Statesman.Analyzers.Tests` reached `total: 125`, not `113`, after both fix rounds and the intervening
Task 6 additions — the measured chain is `95 → 104 → 115 → 121 → 125`; see the Task 5 and Task 7
reports for the detail.

*Amended (Phase 21 fix wave, 2026-09-15):* a sixth bail-out was missing, and this one was in the base
plan's own verbatim code above, not added by either fix round: a local aliased by a `ref` local and
rebound through that alias, `ref var r = ref a; r = new List<int>();`, is a rebind of `a` exactly like
a direct reassignment, but `ref a` is a `RefExpressionSyntax`, which neither the reassignment check nor
the `ref`/`out`/`in` argument check sees. Task 5 and its fix round both missed it; the phase's final
review (Opus, live infrastructure) found it by adversarial probing and measured both rules
false-positiving on unchanged consumer code (`var a = s.Items; ref var r = ref a; r = new List<int>();
a.Add(1);` reported STM004; the STM001 twin on a plain holder reported STM001). Closed by the fix
wave's commit 1: a four-line `RefExpressionSyntax` check immediately after the `ref`/`out` argument
check, two new `Silent` rows (one per rule), and one lever (criterion 6's Lever 23 above).

#### 6. The `event` add/remove asymmetry, documented rather than fixed

`s.Changed += (a, b) => { };`, `s.Changed -= (a, b) => { };` and `s.Plain.Changed += (a, b) => { };`
are silent for both rules, while `s.Plain.Value = 1` on the *same* plain holder reports STM001. The
asymmetry is defensible — subscribing to an event does not change the value the ledger stores, and
reporting would fire on every idiomatic observer wiring — but it is documented nowhere, and a reader
who sees STM001 on a sibling member reads the silence as a bug. **No analyzer change.** One sentence
in the guide's STM001 section, and one pinning fact per rule.

The two rules are silent for **different** reasons, and each fact says which. STM001's silence is
`AnalyzeMutation`'s symbol test, which admits only `IPropertySymbol` and `IFieldSymbol`. STM004's is
its element-access requirement: `+=` **is** one of the assignment kinds it registers for, so
`AnalyzeElementTarget` does run and returns immediately because the target is a member access rather
than an `ElementAccessExpressionSyntax`.

A third guard was found while measuring, and is worth recording because it changes what the levers
prove: the ownership walk's own owner test also admits only properties and fields, so `s.Changed`
resolves to no owner even when the symbol test is widened. Both levers therefore fire on
`s.Plain.Changed += …` alone, out of the three rows — the shape where `Plain` is a property on the
managed `Bag` and the walk can attribute it.

**Measured at `98bf54f`.** `Statesman.Analyzers.Tests` rises from `total: 113` to `total: 119`,
`failed: 0`. Adding `IEventSymbol` to STM001's symbol test fails 1; making STM004's
`AnalyzeElementTarget` admit a member-access target whose symbol is an `IEventSymbol` fails 1.

#### 7. Six documentation corrections, each amended in place

Research section 9 re-ran twelve of Phase 20's close-out claims at `c9cec46`; **all twelve reproduce
to the digit**. Six discrepancies were found in the surrounding documentation, each with a file and a
line. Every one is amended in place with a dated pointer, never silently rewritten.

- **D1 — `docs/guides/analyzers.md`'s STM004 scope bullet is true but materially incomplete.** It
  names `Queue<T>`, `Stack<T>`, the non-generic collections and four missing mutator names. It does
  not mention that the same gap covered every `System.Collections.Concurrent` type except
  `ConcurrentDictionary<,>`, nor — the part that reads as a bug rather than a scope limit — that the
  boundary was partly by **name even on types the rule did analyse**. Item 2 closes the gap, so the
  bullet is rewritten to state what now fires and what the residual boundary is.
- **D2 — an entire false-negative class recorded nowhere.** Item 3.
- **D3 — the key-layout sentence is wrong on three counts**, at `:6099-6101` and repeated at
  `:5439-5441` and `:8163-8164`. "Six key builders" (there are **seven** in
  `Statesman.Persistence.Redis`, plus two in `Statesman.Outbox.Redis`); "two `ITransaction` sites"
  (three `CreateTransaction()` calls, at `RedisStateLedgerStore.cs:315`, `:463` and `:791` — the
  comment at `:458-460` names the first and third, and `PruneAsync` is a third site that inherits the
  constraint without being named); "two Lua scripts take `KEYS` spanning four different keys" (five
  scripts across the two projects, and `AppendScript` alone spans **six** keys). Every error
  understates the work, so the sizing built on it was optimistic.
- **D4 — `:5694`'s "zero occurrences of `List<T>`, `Dictionary<,>` or `HashSet<T>` on a managed type"
  across "this repository's samples, tests and documentation" is false at HEAD.**
  `tests/Statesman.Analyzers.Tests/ManagedStateFixture.cs` declares all three on `Bag`, and Phase 20
  shipped that file. They are deliberately adversarial string-literal fixtures for the analyzer under
  test, not a signal that a consumer wants the rule, so the ruling stands and the sentence gains
  "outside the analyzer's own test fixtures". `docs/guides/analyzers.md`'s STM002 section carries the
  same claim in the same words and is corrected with it.
- **D5 — decision 96's "net saving of four lines" is three.** The declined `AddOrphanedTemporaries`
  helper was written from the three loops it would replace and **does compile**
  (`0 errors, 0 warnings`, `Statesman.FileSystem.Tests` unchanged at `total: 84`); `git diff --stat`
  is `12 insertions(+), 15 deletions(-)`. The body is eight lines as claimed, but the blank line
  separating it from its neighbour makes nine. The conclusion, decline it, is unaffected — three
  lines is a weaker case than four.
- **D6 — no edit needed, and the premise that asked for one was wrong.** The controller's brief for
  the research assumed a "Decisions the user should revisit" section. There is none anywhere at
  `c9cec46`: a recursive search across every markdown file matches only the brief itself. The nearest
  surviving equivalents are this document's "Explicitly parked, with reasons" and the handover's
  "What was parked" paragraph; both were checked item by item and nothing in either is stale beyond
  D3 and D4.

#### 8. `RedisKeyLayout`, an opt-in per-store hash tag

ROADMAP 0.2 bullet 6's large half. Redis Cluster routes a key to a slot by the whole key name, unless
the name contains a hash tag — a substring in braces — in which case only that substring is hashed.

**Only one tag granularity works, and the reason is `AppendScript`.** Every append touches three
per-address keys **and** the three per-store keys in a single `EVAL`, so any tag narrower than the
store leaves that script cross-slot. `CaptureAsync` independently rules out anything narrower,
because it takes one `MULTI`/`EXEC` over head keys for an arbitrary set of addresses.

| Candidate tag | Verdict |
|---|---|
| **Per ledger prefix and store name** | **works.** Every key of one store lands in one slot. |
| Per root | fails. `AppendScript` still spans the store-wide changes, partitions and global-position keys, and `CaptureAsync` spans roots. Making it work needs those three keys to become per-root, a storage-format change that also gives up the global monotonic position across roots — the promise `IStateChangeFeed` makes. |
| Per partition | fails for the same reasons, harder. |

The cost, stated plainly rather than buried: **one store's entire key space sits on one slot, and
therefore on one master node.** A cluster then buys availability and multi-tenancy — many stores
spread across nodes by prefix or name — not per-store write scale-out. That trade-off is inherent in
`AppendScript`'s six-key atomic step, not in the tag.

Keys move. Every stored key changes name, so an existing deployment's data becomes invisible to a
tagged build. `PackageValidationBaselineVersion=0.3.0` does **not** gate this: it compares assembly
surface, not stored data. So the choice is a product decision, and the ruling is a
`RedisKeyLayout KeyLayout` property on `RedisStateLedgerStoreOptions`, defaulting to
`RedisKeyLayout.Legacy` — today's keys byte for byte — with `RedisKeyLayout.SingleSlot` opt-in. The
changelog entry is `### Added`, not `### Breaking`.

Rejected, each with its reason. A **Tooling migration** would have to rename keys under a live writer
with no atomic primitive to do it, and the provider already has export and import, which is the honest
way to move a store. A **documented breaking change** takes a hard break on an unreleased alpha for a
feature nobody has asked for, when an additive enum costs one property. **Making the tag unconditional
later** is strictly worse than shipping the option now, because the option is what lets a consumer opt
in on their own schedule.

The implementation is one private helper, `KeyScope()`, and five key builders repointed at it.
`NotificationChannel()` is deliberately untouched: Redis 7 non-sharded pub/sub is not slot-routed at
all, so a channel name has no slot to share with anything. `Statesman.Outbox.Redis` needs **no change
and gains no option** — every one of its operations is single-key, and it passes 17 of 17 against a
cluster unchanged. That is a genuinely new fact: bullet 6 has been sized as if both Redis packages
needed work.

Three shipped tests build the change-feed key by hand in the untagged shape
(`RedisChangeFeedTests.cs:88`, `:140`, `RedisAppendScriptTests.cs:195`). They are made layout-aware,
not deleted, through the same shared helper the suite uses to pick a layout.

**Measured at `98bf54f`.** The additive enum and property pass the package-validation gate forced on
`Statesman.Persistence.Redis` with `obj/Release` cleared: `APICompat ran successfully without finding
any breaking changes.`, `0 Warning(s) 0 Error(s)`. `Statesman.Redis.Tests` rises from `total: 48` to
`total: 51`, `failed: 0`. Two levers: flipping the default to `SingleSlot` fails the byte-identity
fact; reverting `StreamKey` to the untagged literal fails the single-slot fact.

#### 9. The `redis-cluster` CI job

`redis:7-alpine` can host a single-node cluster, measured — the carried caveat from Phases 19 and 20
is closed. `CLUSTER ADDSLOTS` alone suffices; `redis-cli --cluster create` is not needed for one node
that owns every slot, and `cluster_state:ok` with `cluster_slots_assigned:16384` arrives within a few
seconds.

The job **must** `docker run` the container in a step rather than declare it under `services:`, a
departure from how `redis-tests`, `sqlserver-tests` and `postgres-tests` are all written. Actions
passes a `services:` block only `docker create` options, so it can supply neither the `redis-server`
arguments that enable cluster mode nor the `CLUSTER ADDSLOTS` that has to follow the server coming up.
This was the one structural claim the research reasoned rather than measured; the shell it implies was
composed and run verbatim while this plan was written, and it reaches `cluster_state:ok` and exits 0.

The layout is selected by a new environment variable, `STATESMAN_TEST_REDIS_KEY_LAYOUT`, read in one
place — `tests/Shared/RedisTestLayout.cs`, linked into the three test projects that construct a
`RedisStateLedgerStore`. It selects a layout and never a server: a cluster still needs
`STATESMAN_TEST_REDIS` pointed at it.

Two facts write `Legacy` keys deliberately and cannot run against a cluster at all, so they skip
honestly under `SingleSlot` with that reason, rather than failing or passing silently.

The job joins `pack`'s `needs:` alongside `redis-tests`, `sqlserver-tests` and `postgres-tests`,
because a job nothing depends on gates nothing.

**Measured at `98bf54f`**, against a throwaway single-node cluster, with the RED lever run first:

| Project | Standalone, `Legacy` | Cluster, `SingleSlot` | Cluster, `Legacy` (the lever) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Statesman.Conformance.Tests` | `312` / `0` failed (`288`/`24`) | `312` / **`0`** failed (`288`/`24`) | `312` / **`47`** failed |
| `Statesman.Redis.Tests` | `51` / `0` failed | `51` / **`0`** failed (`49`/`2`) | `51` / **`27`** failed |
| `Statesman.Tooling.Tests` | `24` / `0` failed | `24` / **`0`** failed | `24` / **`2`** failed |
| `Statesman.Outbox.Redis.Tests` | `17` / `0` failed | `17` / **`0`** failed | `17` / `0` failed |

`Statesman.Conformance.Tests` on the cluster under `SingleSlot` is **identical to its standalone
result**. No `MOVED` was observed anywhere, and none can be: with one node owning all 16,384 slots
there is nothing to redirect to, so this configuration exercises `CROSSSLOT` and Lua `KEYS` slot
validation only. A multi-node cluster would be needed for `MOVED`/`ASK`, and that is a materially
bigger job; it is parked with that reason.
